<image-desc>A monochromatic sepia-toned photographic portrait of a man with a handlebar mustache, wearing a high white collar and a dark suit jacket. The portrait is housed within an ornate, occult-themed architectural frame. The top of the frame features a central medallion depicting a profile of an Egyptian figure in a striped headdress <note>This is a "Khat" or "Nemes" headdress, typical of ancient Egyptian royalty</note>, flanked by two large outspread wings. The vertical pillars of the frame are entwined with serpents <note>specifically cobras, or uraei, symbols of sovereignty and divine authority</note> and topped with bearded heads with flames rising from their crowns. The bottom corners of the frame feature two heads wearing turbans and large hoop earrings. In the center of the base pedestal is a stylized facsimile signature.</image-desc>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page serves as a technical "Notice to the Reader" from the 1914 publishers, de Laurence, Scott & Co. It follows the title page and explains the book's unconventional layout and page numbering, which were designed to keep magical illustrations close to their corresponding instructions.</meta>

# Notice To The Reader

To make it easier for the student to understand how to create the <term>Pentacles</term> <note>original: "Pentacles"; these are magical diagrams or symbols, usually circular and inscribed with names of divine power, used in ritual magic to command spirits or provide protection.</note> shown above the illustrations in this book, we found it necessary to rearrange certain pages. If you turn to page 57, you will notice that pages 59 and 60 have been placed between pages 57 and 58, rather than between 58 and 61. As explained above, this was done to ensure the pages containing specific illustrations are near the printed text that explains them. When browsing the book, it may appear at first glance that certain pages are missing; however, upon closer inspection, you will find this is not the case. Although there are no printed page numbers for pages 59, 60, 65, 66, 69, 70, 73, 74, 77, 78, 99, and 100, no text has been omitted. These were illustrated pages showing the <term>Pentacles</term>, and instead of being placed in strict numerical order, they were moved forward or backward to be near the instructions for making them. This arrangement is used throughout the book.

Rather than any pages being missing, you will notice that many pages have actually been added. Several extra pages were inserted using fractional numbers: pages 61½ and 62½ are located between 63 and 64; 63½ is between 67 and 68; 66½ and 67½ are between 71 and 72; 68½ and 69½ are between 75 and 76; and 86½ and 87½ are between 97 and 98. These extra pages were added to make everything clear to the student and to avoid confusion. In studying this book, you will find that the text is complete; not a single word has been left out. All the printed pages have been placed exactly where they belong, while the illustrated pages containing the <term>Pentacles</term>, <term>Seals</term> <note>Magical signatures or graphic symbols representing specific spirits or entities.</note>, and <term>Talismans</term> <note>Objects inscribed with magic symbols intended to provide the wearer with protection or supernatural power.</note> have been placed where they are most convenient for the student.

It should be kept in mind that this famous book is a translation of ancient manuscripts. The way this book has been translated and compiled is a rare literary achievement in the English language. The author has been very meticulous and careful to convey the true meaning of the original manuscripts to the student. This translation shows that the work was not thrown together haphazardly, as is often the case with other translators, but that a complete and logical order runs through the entire volume from beginning to end.

->THE PUBLISHERS.<-

<summary>The publishers explain that the book's non-sequential page numbering is intentional, designed to place magical illustrations near their relevant text, and they reassure the reader that the content is complete and meticulously translated.</summary>

<meta>This page continues from the preface of the work, specifically introducing "Book One" of the Greater Key of Solomon. It establishes the editor's perspective on the text's historical importance and its influence on later occultists like Eliphaz Lévi.</meta>

# The Greater Key Of Solomon

## PREFACE TO BOOK ONE.

In presenting this celebrated magical work to the *student of occultism* <note>original: "Disciple of Occultism"</note>, a few introductory remarks are necessary.

THE KEY OF SOLOMON, except for a shortened and incomplete copy published in France in the seventeenth century, has never yet been printed. Instead, it has for centuries remained in manuscript form, inaccessible to all but the few fortunate scholars who had access to the innermost sections of the great libraries. I therefore consider myself highly honored to be the individual whose task it has been to edit and publish this American edition.

As the source and storehouse of *Kabbalistic magic* <note>original: "Qabalistical Magic"</note> and the origin of much of the *ceremonial magic* of *medieval* <note>original: "Mediæval"</note> times, the “KEY” has always been valued by *occult* writers as a work of the highest authority. Notably in our own time, *Eliphaz Lévi* <note>Alphonse Louis Constant (1810–1875), a hugely influential French ritual magician and author.</note> used it as the model for his celebrated work, “*Dogma and Ritual of High Magic*” <note>original: "Dogme et Rituel de la Haute Magie"</note>. It must be evident to any initiated reader of *Lévi* that THE KEY OF SOLOMON was his primary textbook. At the end of this volume, I have included a fragment of an ancient Hebrew manuscript of THE KEY OF SOLOMON, translated and published in the “*Occult Philosophy*” <note>original: "Philosophie Occulte"</note>, as well as a prayer called the “*Kabbalistic Invocation of Solomon*.” This invocation is very similar to one found in the FIRST BOOK, as it is constructed in the same way using the system of the *Sephiroth* <note>The ten attributes or emanations in Kabbalah through which the Infinite reveals itself.</note>.

The history of the Hebrew original of THE KEY OF SOLOMON is discussed in the introductions, but there is every reason to believe that it has been entirely lost; *Christian* <note>Jean-Baptiste Pitois (1811–1877), who wrote under the name Paul Christian.</note>, the pupil of *Lévi*, says as much in his “*History of Magic*” <note>original: "Histoire de la Magie"</note>.

I see no reason to doubt the tradition that attributes the authorship of the “KEY” to KING SOLOMON. Among others, the Jewish historian *Josephus* specifically mentions the magical works attributed to that monarch. This is confirmed by many Eastern traditions, and Solomon's magical skill is frequently mentioned by the *old adepts* <note>original: "Old Adepts"; highly skilled masters of occult secrets.</note>.

There are, however, two works on *black magic*, “*The True Grimoire*” <note>original: "Grimorium Verum"</note> and “*The Adapted Key of Solomon*” <note>original: "Clavicola di Salomone ridolta"</note>, which have been attrib-

<summary>The editor introduces the first printed American edition of the Key of Solomon, highlighting its historical rarity and its foundational influence on medieval and modern ceremonial magic.</summary>
<keywords>Key of Solomon, Kabbalah, Eliphaz Lévi, Sephiroth, King Solomon, Josephus, ceremonial magic, grimoire</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the editor’s preface, moving from the general history of the text to a discussion of related works, the editor’s ethical stance on "black magic," and the specific manuscripts used for this edition.</meta>

# PREFACE.

...attributed to **SOLOMON**, and which have in some cases been specifically confused with the present work; but they have nothing really to do with it. They are full of evil magic, and I cannot caution the practical student too strongly against them.

There is also another work called the “*Lemegeton, or the Lesser Key of Solomon the King*,” which is full of seals of various spirits; it is not the same as the present book, though it is extremely valuable in its own area of study.

In editing this *volume*, I have omitted one or two experiments that heavily involve **black magic** and which were evidently derived from the two *Goetic* <note>original: "Goetic" — referring to Goetia, a type of magic involving the invocation of spirits or demons, often perceived as lower or darker than theurgical magic.</note> works mentioned above. I must also caution the practical worker against the use of blood; the prayer, the **pentacle**, and the perfumes—or **TEMPLE INCENSE**—are sufficient when used correctly, whereas the former practice verges dangerously toward the path of evil. Let anyone who, despite the warnings in this *volume*, decides to perform evil acts be assured that evil will recoil upon them, and they will be struck by the resulting backlash.

This work is edited from several ancient manuscripts <note>original: "MSS." — abbreviation for manuscripts.</note> in the *British Museum*. These all differ from one another in various ways, with some including what others omit. Unfortunately, they all agree on one thing: the terrible distortion of the **Hebrew** words due to the ignorance of the transcribers. The **Hebrew** is even worse in the *pentacles* <term>Pentacles: Talismans or magical diagrams, often circular, used in ritual magic to focus power or provide protection.</term>, where the letters are so poorly scribbled that they are actually undecipherable in some cases. It has been part of my work for several years to correct and restore the proper **Hebrew** and **magical** characters in these pentacles. The student may therefore safely rely on these being as nearly correct in this current reproduction as possible. Consequently, wherever I could, I have corrected the **Hebrew** of the **magical names** in the *conjurations* and *pentacles*. In the few instances where this was not possible, I have presented them in their most common form, carefully comparing one manuscript with another throughout. The chapters are organized slightly differently in the various manuscripts; in some cases, the material within them has been rearranged. I have added notes wherever necessary.

The manuscripts from which this work is edited are: *Additional MS 10,862*; *Sloane MSS 1307 and 3091*; *Harleian MS 3981*; *King’s MS 288*; and *Lansdowne MSS 1202 and 1203*—seven *codices* <term>Codices: Ancient manuscript texts in book form rather than on scrolls.</term> in all.

Of all these, *Additional MS 10,862* is the oldest, dating to about the end of the sixteenth century. *Harleian MS 3981* is probably from the middle of the seventeenth century, and the others are of a somewhat later date.

*Additional MS 10,862* is written in abbreviated Latin <note>original: "contracted Latin"</note> and is difficult to read, but it contains chapters omitted in the others as well as an important **introduction**. Its wording is more concise. Its title is short: simply **THE KEY OF SOLOMON**, translated from the Hebrew language into Latin <note>original: "translated from the Hebrew language into the Latin."</note>. An exact copy of the writer's signature from this manuscript is shown in *Figure 93*.

*Harleian MS 3981*, *King’s MS 288*, and *Sloane MS 3091* are...

<summary>The editor clarifies the distinction between this work and others like the Lemegeton, warns against black magic and the use of blood, and describes the seven historical manuscripts used to reconstruct the text.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the editor’s description of the specific manuscripts used to compile this edition of "The Key of Solomon," detailing their languages, quality, and varied contents.</meta>

# PREFACE.

These manuscripts are similar and contain the same subject matter with nearly the same wording, but the latter manuscript contains many errors made during copying. They are all written in French. The *Conjurations* <term>Conjurations: Ritual prayers or incantations used to summon or command spirits.</term> and their wording in these versions are much more detailed than in the 10,862 Additional Manuscripts or the 1202 Lansdowne Manuscripts. The title is **THE KEY OF SOLOMON**, King of the Hebrews, translated from the Hebrew language into Italian by Abraham Colorno by the order of his Most Serene Highness of Mantua, and recently translated into French. The *Pentacles* are much better drawn, using colored inks; in the case of the 3091 Sloane Manuscript, gold and silver are used.

The 1307 Sloane Manuscript is in Italian; its title is “*The Little Key of Solomon, Adapted and Summarized in our Native Tongue by the most learned Giovanni Peccatrix*” <note>original: “La Clavicola di Salomone Redotta et epilogata nella nostra materna lingua del dottissimo Gio Peccatrix.”</note>. It is full of **Black Magic** and is a jumble of the proper **KEY OF SOLOMON** and the two black magic books mentioned previously. The *Pentacles* are poorly drawn. However, it does provide part of the introduction found in the 10,862 Additional Manuscripts and is the only other manuscript to do so, except for the beginning of another Italian version bound with it, which bears the title “*Zecorbenei*.”

The 1202 Lansdowne Manuscript is titled “**THE TRUE KEYS OF KING SOLOMON**” by Armadel. It is beautifully written with painted initial letters, and the *Pentacles* are carefully drawn in colored inks. It is more concise in style but omits several chapters. At the end, there are some short extracts from the *True Grimoire* <note>original: “Grimorium Verum”</note> featuring the seals of evil spirits. Since these do not belong to the actual **KEY OF SOLOMON**, I have not included them. According to the clear classification of the “**KEY**,” it consists of two books and no more.

The 1203 Lansdowne Manuscript is “*The Veritable Keys of Solomon*,” translated from Hebrew into Latin by the Rabbi Agognazar. It is written in French in an exquisite printed style, and the *Pentacles* are carefully drawn in colored inks. Although it contains similar material to the others, the arrangement is completely different, as it is all contained in one book and is not even divided into chapters.

The antiquity of the *Planetary Sigils* <term>Sigils: Symbolic representations or "signatures" used in magic to represent specific planetary energies or spirits.</term> is proven by the fact that among the *Gnostic Talismans* in the British Museum, there is a copper ring featuring the *Sigils of Venus*. These are exactly the same as those provided by medieval writers on magic.

Where *Psalms* are referenced, I have in all instances used the English numbering rather than the Hebrew numbering.

In some places, I have substituted the word **AZOTH** <note>Azoth: A term in alchemy representing the universal solvent or the "Alpha and Omega" (the beginning and the end).</note> for “*Alpha and Omega*”—for example, on the blade of the *Knife with the Black Hilt* in *Figure 62*. I should also note that the *Magical Sword* may, in many cases, be used instead of the *Knife*.

In conclusion, for the benefit of those who do not know Hebrew, I will mention that Hebrew is written from right to left. Because the Hebrew alphabet consists mainly of consonants, it requires fewer letters than English to express the same word.

->**L. W. de LAURENCE**<-

*Chicago, Illinois, U.S.A., 1916.*

<summary>This page concludes the preface by cataloging the specific French and Italian manuscripts used for this edition, noting their artistic quality and historical authenticity while explaining the editor's minor textual adjustments.</summary>
<keywords>manuscripts, Sloane MSS, Lansdowne MSS, Abraham Colorno, Giovanni Peccatrix, Armadel, Sigils of Venus, Azoth, Hebrew, translation history</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from a technical description of manuscripts to a biographical and moral introduction to King Solomon, whom the text identifies as the author of the work.</meta>

# SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN"

->BY L. W. de LAURENCE<-

SOLOMON, Son of David and Bathsheba (1033–975 B.C.); King of Israel; noted for his wisdom and deep knowledge of occult forces; author of the "KEY OF SOLOMON."

SOLOMON was a king and the son of a king; the wise son of a wise father; a righteous man's righteous child.

DAVID, the father of SOLOMON, reigned for forty years, as it is written, *"And the days that David governed Israel were forty years."*

Of SOLOMON, it is written, *"And Solomon reigned in Jerusalem over all Israel forty years."*

SOLOMON was born in the year 2912 A.M. <term>A.M.: Anno Mundi, Latin for "Year of the World," a calendar era based on the biblical creation of the world.</term>, and reigned over Israel for forty years. Four hundred and thirty-three years passed between the date of SOLOMON'S reign and the destruction of the *Temple*.

> *"Do you see a man who is diligent in his work? He shall stand before kings; he shall not stand before obscure men."* (Proverbs 22:29.)

In this verse, SOLOMON refers to himself. He built King Solomon's *Temple* in seven years, while he spent fourteen years building his own *Palace*. This was not because his *Palace* was more elegant or more detailed in its craftsmanship than the *Temple*, but because he was diligent in his work to finish the Holy *Temple*, while his own house could wait for time and opportunity.

Four cases comparing the righteousness of fathers and children may be noted:

*First: A righteous man has a righteous son.*

*Second: A wicked man has a wicked son.*

*Third: A wicked man has a righteous son.*

*Fourth: A righteous man has a wicked son.*

To each of these cases, we may find a Biblical reference; to each of them, we may apply a parable and a proverb.

In reference to the righteous father and the righteous son, we find the following verse (Psalm 45:17): *"Your children shall take the place of your fathers."* And we may apply the parable of the good fig tree which brought forth sweet fruit.

In reference to the wicked father and the wicked son, we have in Numbers 32:14: *"And now behold, you have risen up in your fathers' place, a new generation of sinful men."*

Ancient is the proverb, *"Wickedness comes from the wicked"* <note>original: "From the wicked proceedeth wickedness"</note>; and the parable of the serpent bringing forth an asp <term>Asp: A small, venomous snake.</term> is applicable here.

In the third case, the wicked father has a righteous son, as it is

<summary>This page introduces King Solomon as a historical and occult figure, highlighting his diligence in building the Temple and categorizing the moral relationship between fathers and sons using biblical examples.</summary>
<keywords>King Solomon, David, occult forces, King Solomon's Temple, biblical genealogy, Proverbs, Psalms, Numbers, righteousness vs. wickedness</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the comparison between King David and King Solomon, highlighting their shared traits, reigns, and literary contributions, before listing Solomon's expertise in various sciences.</meta>

# SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN."

...is written, "Instead of the thorn shall come up the fir tree." <note>original: Isaiah 55:13</note> And to this we can apply the parable of the rose budding on the bramble bush.

Lastly, a righteous man has a wicked son, as it is written, "*Instead of wheat, thorns came forth.*" (Job 31:40.) <note>The OCR text lists Job 21:40, but the passage "thistle instead of wheat" is found in Job 31:40.</note> And we also have the parable of the attractive peach tree which brought forth bitter fruit.

**SOLOMON** was a king and the son of a king; the wise son of a wise father; a righteous man’s righteous child. All the incidents in **DAVID’S** life and all his characteristics were paralleled in the life of **SOLOMON**.

**DAVID** reigned for forty years, as it is written, "*And the days that David governed Israel were forty years.*"

Of **SOLOMON** it is written, "*And SOLOMON reigned in Jerusalem over all Israel forty years.*" **DAVID** expressed himself through "words," as it is written, "*And these are the last words of David.*"

**SOLOMON** likewise expressed himself through "words."

"*The words of Koheleth, the son of David.*" (Ecclesiastes 1:1.) <term>Koheleth: The Hebrew name for the author of the Book of Ecclesiastes, often translated as "The Preacher" or "The Teacher."</term>

**DAVID** said, "All is vanity"; as it is written, "*For vanity only do all men make a noise.*" (Psalm 39:6.) <note>The OCR lists Psalm 39:7, but "every man walketh in a vain shew" or "vanity" is Psalm 39:6.</note>

**SOLOMON** expressed himself with the same word, "*vanity.*" "*Vanity of vanities, says Koheleth.*" (Ecclesiastes 1:2.)

**David** wrote books, namely: the five books of Psalms; and **SOLOMON** wrote four books: *Proverbs*, *Ecclesiastes*, the *Song of Solomon*, and *The Key of Solomon*.

**David** composed songs: "*And David spoke unto the Lord the words of this song.*" (2 Samuel 22:1.)

**SOLOMON** also composed a song: "*The song of songs,*" which is **SOLOMON’S**.

He was the wise king alluded to in Proverbs 16:23, "*The heart of the wise makes his mouth intelligent, and increases information upon his lips.*" This means that the heart of the wise is full of knowledge and understanding, but this is shown to the world through the words of his mouth. By uttering the thoughts of his mind (or heart) with his lips, he increases the information available to the people. If a man possessing brilliant diamonds and precious stones keeps his jewels concealed, no one is aware of their value; but if he allows them to be seen, their worth becomes known, and the pleasure of ownership is enhanced.

Applying this comparison to the case of **SOLOMON**: while his wisdom was locked up in his own breast, it was of value to no one; but when he had given his four books to the world, men became acquainted with his great abilities. The words of his lips increased the information of his people, and his reputation was so great that anyone in doubt concerning the meaning of a Biblical passage sought the king for an interpretation.

He did not only raise the standard of education in sacred lore. He had mastered and taught the sciences of *Natural Philosophy*, *Physiology*, *Botany*, *Agriculture*, *Mathematics* in all its branches, *Occultism*, *Astronomy*, and *Chemistry*—in fact, all useful studies. He also taught *Rhetoric*. <term>Natural Philosophy: The study of nature and the physical universe, the precursor to modern science.</term> <term>Occultism: The study of hidden or supernatural powers and the laws of the universe.</term>

# SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN."

...and the rules of poetry <note>original: "Poesy"</note>. In *occultism* and "*talismanic magic*" he was an *expert* <note>original: "Adept"</note>.

And in addition to the fact that Koheleth <term>Koheleth: The Hebrew name for the author of Ecclesiastes, often identified as Solomon</term> was wise, he continually taught the people knowledge.

If what others said interested the people, how much more readily did they listen to SOLOMON; with how much more ease did they understand him!

We may illustrate his method of teaching by the following comparison: There was a basket without handles <note>original: "ears"</note>, filled with fine fruit, but the owner was unable to get it to his home because of the difficulty in carrying it, until a wise man, seeing the predicament, attached handles to the basket, after which it could be carried with great ease.

So did SOLOMON remove difficulties from the path of the student. 

Rabbi Huna further illustrated this same concept. "There was once," he said, "a well of the most pure and excellent water; but the well was so deep that the people were not able to reach the water, until a man of wisdom, taking a bucket, attached to it one rope after another until the whole was long enough to reach the water. So was it with SOLOMON'S teachings. The Bible is a well of truth, but its teachings are too deep for the understanding of some. SOLOMON, however, introduced parables and proverbs suited to the comprehension of all, through which means a knowledge of the law became readily obtainable."

Rabbi Simon, the son of Chalafta, related the following parable: "A certain king had an officer to whom he was much attached, and whom he took great delight in honoring. One day he said to this favorite, 'Come, express a wish; anything that I can give you shall be yours.' Then this officer thought, 'If I ask the king for gold or silver or precious stones, he will give what I ask; even though I desire higher honor and a more exalted station he will grant it; yet I will ask him for his daughter, for if he grants that, all the rest will be included.'"

When the Lord appeared to SOLOMON in Gibeon <note>original: "Gibon"</note>, and said to him in a dream, "*What shall I give to you?*" SOLOMON reflected, "*If I ask for gold, silver, or jewels, the Lord will give them to me; I will ask, however, for wisdom; if that is granted to me, all other good things are included.*" Therefore, he replied, "*Give to your servant an understanding heart.*"

Then the Lord said:

> "Because you have asked for wisdom, and requested not wealth or dominion over your enemies; by your life, wisdom and knowledge shall be yours, and through them you shall obtain wealth and power."

> "And Solomon awoke, and behold, it was a dream." He wandered into the fields, and he heard the voices of the animals; the donkey brayed, the lion roared, the dog barked, the rooster crowed, and behold, he understood what they said to one another.

An ox, even after being killed and prepared for meat <note>original: "dressed"</note>, may be made to stand, provided the tendons <note>original: "sinews"</note> are uncut; but if they are severed, ropes are required...

<meta>This page explores King Solomon's later life, his reflections on the transience of existence (the "vanity" of man's stages), his diplomatic wit with the King of Egypt, and the resolution of theological contradictions in his writings.</meta>

...to hold the body together. While Solomon remained free from sin, his prayers were granted for his own sake. But when he departed from the righteous way, the Lord said to him, “*For the sake of David, my servant, I will not take the kingdom from you in your lifetime.*”

Solomon said, “*Vanity of vanities; vanity, even as a shadow.*” What kind of shadow? The shadow of a tower or a tree remains a shadow for a while and then is lost, but the shadow of a bird flies away, and there is neither bird nor shadow. David said, “*Our days are like a passing shadow,*” and Rabbi Huna said, “*Our days pass quickly from us, even as the shadow of a flying bird.*”

With the word "vanity," Solomon expresses seven stages of a man’s life.

The infant he compares to a king, riding in his little carriage <note>original: "coach"</note> and being kissed, admired, and praised by all. The child of three or four years he compares to a pig, fond of dirt and soiling itself with its food. The child of ten is fond of dress; the youth adorns himself and seeks a wife; the married man is as bold as a dog in seeking a livelihood for himself and his family; and the old man he likens to an ape.

->“*God gave Wisdom to Solomon.*”<-

When Solomon was preparing to build the Temple, he applied to the King of *Egypt* for men to help him in the work. *Pharaoh*, consulting his *astrologers*, selected those men who were destined to die within the year. When they arrived at *Jerusalem*, the wise Solomon sent them back at once. With each man, he sent a burial shroud <note>original: "shroud"</note> and directed them to say to their master, “If *Egypt* is too poor to supply shrouds for her dead, and for that purpose sends them to me, look—here they are: the men and the shrouds together. Take them and bury your dead.”

He was wiser than all other men, wiser even than Adam, who gave names to all the animals of the world and even to himself, saying, “*From the dust of the ground I was formed, and therefore shall my name be Adam.*” <note>The name Adam is related to the Hebrew word *adamah*, meaning "ground" or "earth."</note> Rabbi Tanchum said, “Where is your wisdom and your understanding, O King Solomon? Your words not only contradict themselves but also the words of David, your father. He said, ‘*The dead cannot praise the Lord*’ (Psalm 115:17), yet you said, ‘*Thereupon I praised the dead who are already dead, more than the living who are still alive*’ (*Ecclesiastes* 4:2). And you also said, ‘*For a living dog fares better than a dead lion.*’” (*Ecclesiastes* 9:4).

These seeming contradictions, however, may be readily explained. David said, “The dead cannot praise the Lord,” meaning that we should study God’s law during life, as after its end <note>original: "cessation"</note> it would be impossible. Solomon said, “Thereupon I praised the dead who are already dead.” When the children of Israel sinned in the wilderness, Moses prayed for them for their own sakes, and his prayer was unanswered; but when he said, “*Remember Abraham, Isaac, and Israel, Your servants,*” he met with a prompt reply. Therefore, did Solomon not speak well in saying—

### SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN."

“Praise the dead who are already dead”? <note>original: “Praise the dead that are already dead”</note> Consider another instance: A king may issue laws, but many of his subjects may ignore them. Sometimes these laws, even if they are earnestly followed during the life of the person who made them, may be repealed or become outdated after his death. MOSES, however, established many strict laws which have been followed throughout all generations. Therefore, SOLOMON was right to say, “*Thereupon will I praise the dead.*”

Rabbi Judah, speaking in the name of Rab, further explained this verse. He asked, “What is the meaning of the following passage? ‘Show me a sign of Your favor, so that those who hate me may see it and be ashamed.’ (*Psalm 86:17*) <note>The original text cites Psalm 76:17, but this verse appears in Psalm 86 in modern Bibles.</note> DAVID said to God, after his sin with Bathsheba (*2 Samuel 11-12*), ‘Lord of the Universe, forgive me for my sin.’ The Lord answered, ‘I will forgive you.’ Then DAVID said, ‘Show me the sign during my lifetime,’ but God said, ‘Not in your lifetime, but in the lifetime of SOLOMON, your son, will I show it.’ 

Thus, when SOLOMON dedicated the Temple, even though he prayed with fervent devotion, he was not answered until he said, ‘O Lord God, do not reject your anointed one. Remember the faithful deeds of DAVID, your servant.’ (*2 Chronicles 6:42.*) Then he was quickly answered, for in the next verse we read, ‘And when SOLOMON had finished praying, fire came down from Heaven and consumed the burnt offering and the sacrifices, and the glory of the Lord filled the house.’ Then the enemies of DAVID were put to shame, for all Israel knew that God had forgiven DAVID for his sin. Did not SOLOMON say well then, ‘Thereupon praised I the dead?’ For this reason, further on in the chapter we read, ‘And on the twenty-third day of the seventh month he sent the people back to their homes <note>original: "unto their tents"</note>, joyful and glad in their hearts, because of the good that the Lord had done for DAVID, and for SOLOMON, and for ISRAEL, His people.’”

SOLOMON said, “For a living dog is better off than a dead lion.”

Explaining this verse, Rabbi Judah said, in the name of Rab, “What is the meaning of the verse, ‘Let me know, O Lord, my end, and the measure of my days, what it is; I wish to know when I shall cease to be.’ (*Psalm 39:4.*)

“David said to God, ‘Let me know, O Lord, my end.’ God answered, ‘I have decreed that for every person, their end must remain hidden in the future.’ Then DAVID said, ‘What is the measure of my days?’ Again God replied, ‘No man may know the measure of his days.’ ‘I wish to know when I shall cease to be,’ continued DAVID; and God answered, ‘You will die on a Sabbath.’

“‘Let me die the day after,’ begged DAVID, but the Lord answered, ‘No; then the kingdom will belong to SOLOMON, and one reign may not take away from another reign even by a hair’s breadth.’ ‘Then let me die the day before,’ David exclaimed, ‘for one day spent in Your courts is better than a thousand elsewhere,’ and God said, ‘One day spent by you in...’

### SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN."

“...studying my law is more acceptable than the thousand burnt offerings your son SOLOMON will sacrifice.”

> “It was DAVID’s custom to spend every Sabbath studying the Bible and its commandments, and he was so engaged on the Sabbath that was to be his last. Behind the king’s palace there was an orchard; DAVID, hearing a noise there, walked out to find its cause. Upon entering the orchard, he fell to the ground and died.
>
> “The noise in the orchard had been caused by the barking of the king’s dogs, who had not been fed that day. SOLOMON sent a message to the Rabbinical College <note>original: “Rabbinical College”; likely referring to the Sanhedrin or a gathering of sages</note>, saying, ‘My father lies dead in his orchard; is it permissible to move his body on the Sabbath? My father’s dogs are begging for their food; is it proper to cut meat for them today?’ The college returned this answer: ‘Your father’s body should not be moved today, but give meat to the dogs.’ Therefore, SOLOMON said, ‘A living dog fares better than a dead lion,’ justly comparing the son of Jesse to that king of beasts.” <note>See Ecclesiastes 9:4.</note>

SOLOMON was the chosen of the Lord, who named him, through the mouth of Nathan the prophet, *Yedidiah* (the beloved of God) <note>original: *Yedidiah*</note>. He was called SOLOMON (meaning "peace") because in his days peace reigned, as it is written, “And Judah and Israel lived in safety.” <note>See 1 Kings 4:25 (cited as 1 Kings 5:5 in the text).</note> He was called *Ithiel* (meaning "God is with me") <note>original: *Ithiel*</note> because God was his support.

When SOLOMON sat upon the throne of his father DAVID, all the nations of the earth feared him; all the people of the earth listened anxiously for his words of wisdom.

Afterward, he had a throne made especially for himself by *Hiram*, the son of a widow of *Tyre*. It was covered with gold from *Ophir* and set with all kinds of precious and valuable stones. The seat of the throne was reached by six broad steps. The right side of the first step was guarded by an ox made of pure gold, and the left side by a lion of the same metal. On the right of the second step stood a bear, also of gold, and on the left a lamb—symbolizing enemies living in peace together. On the right of the third step was placed a golden camel, and on the left an eagle. On the right of the fourth step there was also an eagle with outspread wings, and on the left a bird of prey, all made of the same precious metal. On the fifth step to the right, a golden cat was fashioned in a crouching position, and on the left, a chicken. On the right of the sixth step, a hawk was fashioned, and on the left side a pigeon; upon the very top of the step, a pigeon clutched a hawk in her talons. These animals were designed to represent the time when those of opposite natures shall unite in harmony, as it is written in *Isaiah* (11:6), “And the wolf shall then live with the sheep.”

Over the throne hung a golden chandelier with seven branches. It was ornamented with roses, knobs, bowls, and tongs; on the seven branches, the names of the seven patriarchs—*Adam*, *Noah*, *Shem*, *Abraham*, *Isaac*, *Jacob*, and *Job*—were engraved.

On the second row of the chandelier's branches were engraved—

### SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN."

...the names of the seven pious ones of the world: *Levi*, *Kehath*, *Amram*, *Moses*, *Aaron*, *Eldad*, and *Madad*. Above all this hung a golden urn <note>original: "churn"; likely a transcription error for "urn" or a reference to a vessel for the anointing oil</note> filled with pure olive oil, and on this were engraved the names of *Eli*, the *High Priest*, and his two sons, *Hophni* and *Phineas*; and on the other side, the names of the two sons of *Aaron*, *Nadab* and *Abihu*.

On the right hand of the throne two chairs were placed, one for the *High Priest* and the other for the *Vice-High Priest* <term>the Sagan, or deputy High Priest</term>, and upon the left side, from the top to the ground, seventy-one chairs were stationed as seats for the members of the <term>Sanhedrin</term> <note>original: "Sanhedrim"; the supreme judicial council of ancient Israel</note>.

The throne was made upon wheels, so that it could be moved easily wherever the king might desire it to be.

The Lord gave SOLOMON the power of understanding the nature and properties of the plants <note>original: "herbs"</note> of the field and the trees of the forest, as it is written: “And he spoke concerning the trees, from the cedar tree that is upon Lebanon even unto the hyssop that springs out of the wall. He spoke also concerning the beasts, and concerning the birds, and concerning the creeping things, and concerning the fishes.” <note>See 1 Kings 4:33; cited as 1 Kings 5:13 in the text.</note>

It is said that SOLOMON ruled the whole world, and this verse is quoted as proof of the claim: “And SOLOMON was ruling over all the kingdoms, which brought presents, and served SOLOMON all the days of his life.” <note>See 1 Kings 4:21; cited as 1 Kings 5:1 in the text.</note>

All the kingdoms congratulated SOLOMON as the worthy successor of his father, DAVID, whose fame was great among the nations—all except one: the kingdom of *Sheba*, the capital of which was called *Kitore* <note>original: "Kitore"; likely referring to Kitor, a legendary city mentioned in Rabbinic accounts of Sheba</note>.

To this kingdom SOLOMON sent a letter:

> “From me, King SOLOMON, peace to you and to your government. Let it be known to you that the Almighty God has made me to reign over the whole world: the kingdoms of the *North*, the *South*, the *East*, and the *West*. Look, they have come to me with their congratulations, all except you alone.
>
> “Come also, I pray you, and submit to my authority, and much honor shall be done for you; but if you refuse, behold, I shall by force compel your acknowledgment.
>
> “To you, *Queen Sheba*, is addressed this letter in peace from me, King SOLOMON, the son of DAVID.”

Now when *Queen Sheba* received this letter, she sent in haste for her elders and counselors to ask their advice as to the nature of her reply.

They spoke only lightly of the message and the one who sent it, but the queen did not regard their words. She sent a vessel carrying many presents of different metals, minerals, and precious stones to SOLOMON. It was after a voyage of two years’ time that these presents arrived at Jerusalem, and in a letter entrusted to the captain, the queen said, “After...

# SOLOMON, THE "WISE MAN."

"...you have received the message, then I myself will come to you." And two years after this time, the *Queen of Sheba* arrived at *Jerusalem*.

When SOLOMON heard that the queen was coming, he sent *Benaiah* <note>original: "Benayahu"</note>, the son of *Jehoiada* <note>original: "Yehoyadah"</note>, the general of his army, to meet her. When the queen saw him, she thought he was the king, and she stepped down <note>original: "alighted"</note> from her carriage.

Then *Benaiah* asked, "Why do you step down from your carriage?"
And she answered, "Are you not his majesty, the king?"
"No," replied *Benaiah*, "I am but one of his officers."

Then the queen turned back and said to her ladies-in-waiting, "If this is only one of the officers, and he is so noble and imposing in appearance, how great must be his superior, the king!"

And *Benaiah*, the son of *Jehoiada*, conducted the *Queen of Sheba* to the palace of the king.

SOLOMON prepared to receive his visitor in a suite <note>original: "apartment"</note> floor-tiled and lined with glass; the queen was at first so deceived by the appearance that she imagined the king to be sitting in water. <note>This refers to a famous legend where the glass floor was so clear it looked like a pool of water, leading the queen to lift her skirts.</note>

And when the queen had tested SOLOMON's wisdom and witnessed his magnificence, she said:

> "I did not believe what I heard, but now I have come, and my eyes have seen it all; behold, the half has not been told to me. Happy are your servants who stand before you continually to listen to your words of wisdom. Blessed be the Lord your God, who has placed you on a throne to rule righteously and in justice."

When other kingdoms heard the words of the *Queen of Sheba*, they feared SOLOMON exceedingly, and he became greater than all the other kings of the earth in wisdom and in wealth.

SOLOMON was born in the year 2912 <term>A.M.</term> <note>original: "Anno Mundi," meaning "in the year of the world," a calendar system based on the biblical creation</note>, and reigned over *Israel* forty years. Four hundred and thirty-three years passed between the date of Solomon's reign and that of the *Temple's* destruction.

# INTRODUCTION.

->*From Additional Manuscript 10862, “The Key of Solomon,” translated into Latin from the Hebrew style.* <note>original: "idiom"</note><-

Treasure my words, my son Roboam, since I, Solomon, have received this wisdom from the Lord.

Then Roboam answered and said: "How have I deserved to follow the example of my father Solomon in such things? For he has been found worthy to receive the knowledge of all living things through the teaching of an Angel of God."

And Solomon said: "Hear, my son, and receive my sayings; learn the wonders of God. For on a certain night, when I lay down to sleep, I called upon that most holy Name of God, **IAH** <note>A shortened form of Yahweh, the name of God in the Hebrew Bible.</note>, and prayed for the *Ineffable Wisdom*. 

As I was beginning to close my eyes, the Angel of the Lord, Homadiel, appeared to me and spoke many things courteously to me. He said: 'Listen, Solomon! Your prayer before the Most High is not in vain. Since you have asked neither for long life, nor for great riches, nor for the souls of your enemies, but have asked for wisdom for yourself to perform justice, the Lord says this: According to your word, I have given you a wise and understanding heart, so that there was no one like you before you, nor shall any like you ever arise.'

When I comprehended the speech made to me, I understood that I possessed the knowledge of all creatures—both things in the heavens and things beneath the heavens. I saw that all the writings and wisdom of this present age were empty and futile <note>original: "vain"</note>, and that no man was perfect. 

I composed a certain work in which I detailed <note>original: "rehearsed"</note> the 'secret of secrets,' where I have kept them hidden. In it, I have also concealed every secret of the magical arts used by any masters; I have included every secret or experiment of these sciences that is in any way worth accomplishing. I have written them in this 'Key,' so that just as a key opens a treasure-house, so this *Key* alone may open the knowledge and understanding of magical arts and sciences.

Therefore, my son, you may see every experiment of mine or of others, provided everything is properly prepared for them. You must follow what I have set down regarding the proper day, hour, and all necessary things; without this, there will be nothing but falsehood and worthlessness <note>original: "vanity"</note> in this work of mine. Within this book are hidden all secrets and mysteries which can be performed. What I have written concerning a single <term>divination</term> <note>The practice of seeking knowledge of the future or the unknown by supernatural means.</note> or a single experiment, I believe to be true for all things in the Universe—those which have been, and those which shall be in the future.

Therefore, my son Roboam, I command you by the blessing you expect from your father: you shall make an ivory casket, and inside it you shall place, keep, and hide this 'Key' of mine. When I have passed away to join my ancestors <note>original: "passed away unto my fathers"</note>, I beg you to place the casket in my tomb."

<summary>King Solomon recounts a vision of the angel Homadiel, who granted him divine wisdom, which Solomon then recorded in this "Key" to be inherited by his son Roboam. Solomon instructs his son to keep the book in an ivory casket and eventually bury it with him.</summary>
<keywords>Solomon, Roboam, Homadiel, Key of Solomon, grimoire, divine wisdom, magical arts, divination, ivory casket</keywords>

# INTRODUCTION.

...beside me, so that at another time it might not fall into the hands of the wicked. And as **SOLOMON** commanded, so was it done.

And when, therefore, men had waited for a long time, certain *Babylonian Philosophers* came to the *Sepulchre* <note>A tomb or burial chamber.</note>; and when they had assembled, they at once took counsel together that a certain number of men should renovate the *Sepulchre* in his (**SOLOMON’S**) honor. When the *Sepulchre* was dug out and repaired, the *Ivory Casket* was discovered, and inside was the *Key of Secrets*. They took this with joyful minds, but when they had opened it, none among them could understand it because of the obscurity of the words and their *occult* <note>original: "occult," meaning hidden, secret, or supernatural</note> arrangement, and the hidden character of the meaning and knowledge; for they were not worthy to possess this treasure.

Then, therefore, one arose among them who was more worthy than the others, both in the sight of the gods and by reason of his age, who was called *Iohé Grevis*. He said to the others: "Unless we shall come and ask for the interpretation from the Lord with tears and entreaties, we shall never arrive at the knowledge of it."

Therefore, when each of them had retired to his bed, *Iohé* fell upon his face on the earth and began to weep, striking his breast and saying:

> What have I done to deserve this above others, seeing that so many men can neither understand nor interpret this knowledge, even though there were no secret thing in nature which the Lord has hidden from me? Why are these words so obscure? Why am I so ignorant?

And then on his bended knees, stretching his hands toward Heaven, he said:

> O God, the Creator of all, You who know all things, Who gave such great wisdom to **SOLOMON THE SON OF DAVID THE KING**; grant to me, I beseech You, O Holy Omnipotent and Ineffable <note>Too sacred or great to be expressed in words.</note> Father, the power to receive that wisdom, so that I may become worthy by Your aid to attain the understanding of this *Key of Secrets*.

And immediately the *Angel of the Lord* appeared to me <note>The text shifts to the first-person perspective of Iohé Grevis.</note>, saying:

> Remember that if the secrets of **SOLOMON** appear hidden and obscure to you, the Lord has wished it so, so that such wisdom may not fall into the hands of wicked men. Therefore, do you promise me that you are not willing for such great wisdom to ever come to any living creature? And whatever you reveal to anyone, let them know that they must keep it to themselves; otherwise the secrets are profaned and no effect can follow.

And *Iohé* answered: "I promise to you that I will reveal them to no one, except for the honor of the Lord, and with much discipline, unto penitent, secretive, and faithful persons."

Then the Angel answered: "Go and read the 'Key,' and its words, which were obscure throughout, shall be made manifest <note>Clearly revealed or easy to understand.</note> to you."

# INTRODUCTION.

After this, the angel ascended into Heaven in a flame of fire. Then *Iohé* <note>Referring to Iohé Grevis, the legendary Babylonian philosopher introduced on the previous page.</note> was glad. Working with a clear mind, he understood what the angel of the Lord had said, and he saw that THE KEY OF SOLOMON was transformed so that it appeared plainly and clearly to him in all its parts. *Iohé* realized that this work might fall into the hands of the ignorant, and he said: "I command him into whose hands this secret may come—by the power of the Creator and His wisdom—that in all things he may desire, intend, and perform, he must ensure that this treasure comes to no unworthy person, nor should he reveal it to anyone who is unwise or who does not fear God. Because if he acts otherwise, I pray to God that he may never be worthy to achieve the desired result."

And so he deposited the "Key," which SOLOMON had preserved, back into the ivory casket. The words of the "Key" are as follows, divided into TWO BOOKS and presented in order.

### AN ADMONISHMENT BY &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp; de LAURENCE.

In presenting this translation of a most ancient and historical manuscript—now held in the British Museum in London—to the student of *Occultism*, a warning is necessary. <insert>Death</insert> <note>This insertion appears abruptly in the original text, likely as a grave warning regarding the misuse of the rituals.</note>

For twenty years, the writer has received many hundreds of urgent requests from serious investigators and advanced *occult* students for an authentic and official copy of "*The Key of SOLOMON, Son of David, King of Israel.*" Among these students, those making the request belong to the class who are intensely interested in the production of such *seals* <note>original: "Seals," symbolic diagrams believed to have magical properties</note> and *charms* as are used in various operations and experiments. I must, however, warn the reader that if he wishes to succeed in these matters and have his desires fulfilled by being able to perform these operations, it will be absolutely necessary for him to first arrange all essential things and to observe and practice the instructions contained here.

To be brief, it will be necessary for the disciple to exercise care and observation, and to abstain from all unlawful things and every kind of wickedness. Furthermore, the disciple should put the operations into practice exactly as they are set down here. Therefore, let it be understood that there is nothing further to add to the instructions contained here, as all the information that was contained in *King* SOLOMON’S *manuscripts* is included in this volume. Let the student study it seriously and with proper meditation; his mind will be enlightened, and he will advance by degrees. But under no circumstances can he expect to progress without serious study and deep meditation. Let the reader realize that learning the work of the Old Masters—and such great men as SOLOMON, who in his time became the *King of Israel*—is no small task and requires faithful application.

<summary>The legendary introduction concludes with Iohé Grevis hiding the now-intelligible "Key" from the unworthy, followed by L.W. de Laurence's editorial warning that success in occult operations requires moral purity and rigorous study.</summary>

# INTRODUCTION.

->From the Lansdowne Manuscript 1203, "The True Keys of Solomon,"<-
->*translated from Hebrew into Latin by Rabbi Abognazar.*<-
<note>original: "The Veritable Clavicles of Solomon." The word "clavicle" is derived from the Latin *clavicula*, meaning a small key.</note>

O my son, Rehoboam! <note>original: "Roboam"</note> Since there is no science more useful than the knowledge of *celestial movements*, I have thought it my duty—being at the point of death—to leave you an inheritance more precious than all the riches I have enjoyed. In order for you to understand how I arrived at this degree of wisdom, I must tell you that one day, while I was meditating on the power of the *Supreme Being*, the angel of the great God appeared before me. As I was saying, "Oh, how wonderful are the works of God!" I suddenly saw, at the end of a dense, shaded grove of trees, a light in the form of a blazing star. It spoke to me with a voice of thunder: "SOLOMON, SOLOMON, do not be dismayed; the Lord is willing to satisfy your desire by giving you knowledge of whatever thing is most pleasing to you. I order you to ask of Him whatever you desire." Whereupon, recovering from my surprise, I answered the angel that, according to the will of the Lord, I only desired the gift of wisdom. By the grace of God, I obtained not only that, but also the enjoyment of all *celestial* <note>meaning: heavenly or related to the stars</note> treasures and the knowledge of all natural things.

It is by this means, my son, that I possess all the virtues and riches you see me enjoying now. So that you will be attentive to everything I am about to relate to you, and so that you will carefully remember all that I tell you, I assure you that the graces of the Great God will become familiar to you. The *celestial* and *terrestrial* <note>meaning: earthly</note> *creatures* will be obedient to you. This is a science that operates only through the strength and power of natural things and by the pure angels who govern them. I will give you the names of these angels in order, along with their specific tasks and the particular duties to which they are destined, together with the days over which they specifically preside. This is so that you may accomplish everything you find in this, my *testament*. I promise you success in all of this, provided that all your works tend only toward the honor of God, who has given me the power to rule not only over *terrestrial* but also over *celestial* things—that is to say, over the angels, whom I am able to command according to my will to obtain significant services from them.

*First.* It is necessary for you to understand that God, having made all things so they may be submitted to Him, wished to bring His works to perfection by making one creature who participates in both the divine and the *terrestrial*: that is to say, Man. Man's body is physical <note>original: "gross"</note> and earthly, while his soul is spiritual and heavenly. God has made the whole earth and its inhabitants subject to man, and has given him the means by which he may make the angels familiar <note>meaning: to bring spirits into one's service or close acquaintance</note> to him. I call those *celestial* creatures "angels" who are destined for various tasks: some to regulate the motion of the stars, others to inhabit the elements, others to aid and direct men, and

<summary>King Solomon addresses his son Rehoboam, explaining that his wisdom and power over angels were divine gifts granted after an angelic encounter. He begins to explain the dual nature of man and the roles of the various angels that can be commanded through this "testament."</summary>

<header>INTRODUCTION.</header>

...others again to sing continually the praises of the Lord. You may then, by the use of their Seals and Characters <note>Occult symbols or signatures used to represent and summon specific spirits.</note>, make them familiar to you, provided that you do not abuse this privilege by demanding things from them which are contrary to their nature. For accursed be he who takes the Name of God in vain, and who employs for evil purposes the knowledge and goodness with which He has enriched us.

*I command\* you, my son, to carefully engrave in your memory all that I say to you, so that it may never leave you. If you do not intend to use the secrets which I teach you here for a good purpose, I command you to throw this Testament into the fire, rather than abuse the power you will have to compel <note>original: "constraining"</note> the spirits. I warn you that the benevolent <note>original: "beneficent"</note> angels—tired and worn out by your illegal <note>original: "illicit"</note> demands—would execute God's commands to your own sorrow, just as they would for anyone who, with evil intent, would abuse those secrets which He has given and revealed to me. However, do not think, my son, that you are forbidden from profiting from the good fortune and happiness which the Divine Spirits can bring you. On the contrary, it gives them great pleasure to serve humanity, for whom many of these spirits have a great liking and affinity, God having destined them for the protection and guidance of those earthly things which are submitted to the power of man.*

There are different kinds of spirits, according to the things over which they preside. Some of them govern the *Empyrean Heaven* <note>The highest part of heaven, believed in ancient cosmology to be the home of God and pure light.</note>, others the *First Mover* <note>original: "Primum Mobilé," the outermost moving sphere of the universe in the Ptolemaic system.</note>, others the *First* and *Second Crystalline* <note>Transparent spheres believed to surround the Earth.</note>, and others the *Starry Heaven*. There are also spirits of the Heaven of Saturn, whom I call *Saturnites*. There are spirits of Jupiter <note>original: "Jovial"</note>, Mars <note>original: "Martial"</note>, the Sun <note>original: "Solar"</note>, Venus <note>original: "Venerean"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "Mercurial"</note>, and the Moon <note>original: "Lunar"</note>. There are also spirits in the Elements as well as in the Heavens: some in the Region of Fire, others in the Air, others in the Water, and others upon the Earth. All of these can serve the man who learns their nature and knows how to attract them.

Furthermore, I want you to understand that God has assigned to each one of us a spirit which watches over us and takes care of our protection. These are called *Genii* <note>Guardian spirits or tutelary deities; the singular is "Genius."</note>. They are elemental like we are, and they are more ready to serve those whose temperament matches the *Element* in which these *Genii* live. For example, should you be of a fiery temperament—that is to say, "sanguine" <note>One of the four traditional "humors" or personality types, historically associated with blood and a courageous or energetic nature.</note>—your genius would be fiery and submitted to the *Empire of Bael* <note>A powerful spirit or king often mentioned in Grimoires.</note>. Besides this, there are special times reserved for the invocation of these spirits, during the days and hours when they have power and absolute rule. It is for this reason that you will see in the following tables which Planet and which Angel each Day and Hour is submitted to, together with the colors which belong to them, and the metals, herbs, plants, and aquatic, aerial, and land animals associated with them. This also includes the temple incense proper to each of them, as well as the direction of the universe <note>The four cardinal directions: North, South, East, and West.</note> toward which they ask to be called. Neither are the conjurations, seals, characters, and divine letters omitted, through which we receive the power to connect with these spirits.

| **Archangel** | Tzaphqiel <note>Also spelled Zaphkiel.</note> | Tzadiquel <note>Also spelled Zadkiel.</note> | Khamael <note>Also spelled Camael.</note> | Raphael | Haniel | Michael | Gabriel |

---

->NOTE BY DE LAURENCE <note>Lauron William de Laurence (1868–1936) was an American author and publisher of occult texts who often edited and reissued older grimoires.</note><-

These tables have been cross-referenced and compared with various examples, both in manuscript and printed form. They are to be used as follows: Suppose the student wishes to discover the properties of the hour from 12:00 to 1:00 p.m. on a Tuesday. First, look in the "*Table of the Planetary Hours*" [on the previous page]. Having found the hour marked "1" in the column titled "Hours from Midnight to Midnight," the student will see the figure "8" on the same line in the column titled "Hours from Sunset to Sunset," showing it to be the eighth hour of the day. In the column titled "Tuesday," the name "Mars" appears, showing that this hour is under the dominion of the planet Mars. 

Upon consulting the "*Table of the Magical Names of the Hours*," the student will find the name *Beron* under the number 1. In the "Tuesday" column on the same line, the name of the angel *Zamael* appears, showing that the ruler of the hour is the Angel *Zamael* and that its magical name is *Beron*. Furthermore, by referring to the third table [on this page], the student will see that Tuesday is under the rule of the planet Mars, whose Archangel is *Khamael*, Angel is *Zamael*, metal is *iron*, and color is *red*. 

Similarly, it will be found that the hour from 10:00 to 11:00 p.m. on Saturday is the sixth hour of the night and is under the dominion of the Sun. Its magical name is *Cäerra*, and the Angel *Michael* rules it. Saturday itself is under the dominion of the Archangel *Tzaphqiel*, the Angel *Cassiel*, and the planet Saturn; the metal *lead* and the color *black* are associated with it.

The following text is taken from the following manuscripts <note>These refer to specific historical documents held in the British Library.</note>, which have been cross-referenced and compared with each other:

Sloane MSS. 1307; Sloane MSS. 3091; Harleian MSS. 3981; Additional MSS. 10862; King’s MSS. 288; Lansdowne MSS. 1202.

Extracts have also been made from Lansdowne MSS. 1203, which differs considerably from the others in its general arrangement, though it contains very similar subject matter.

In cases where the manuscripts varied from each other, I have chosen the version that seemed most likely to be correct, occasionally mentioning the different readings in footnotes. I have also corrected the Hebrew names in the incantations, for these were sometimes so distorted as to be hardly recognizable; for example, "Zenard" was often written instead of *Tzabaoth* <note>original: "Zenard, written for Tzabaoth." Tzabaoth is a Hebrew title for God meaning "of Hosts."</note>, and so on.

<meta>This page serves as a "Preliminary Discourse" or introduction to the text. It provides a legendary history of the manuscript, tracing its lineage from King Solomon to his son Roboam, then through Rabbi Abognazar to the Archbishop of Arles, establishing the supposed authenticity of the grimoire.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>
# PRELIMINARY DISCOURSE

->From *Lansdowne Manuscripts 1203*, “*The True Keys of Solomon*,”<-
->*translated from Hebrew into the Latin language*<-
->*by Rabbi Abognazar.*<-

---

Everyone knows today that from time immemorial, Solomon possessed knowledge inspired by the wise teachings of an angel.* He appeared so submissive and obedient to these teachings that, in addition to the gift of wisdom which he requested, he obtained an abundance of all other virtues. This occurred so that knowledge worthy of eternal preservation would not be buried with his body. Being near the end of his life, so to speak, he left his son *Roboam* a *Testament* which contained all the wisdom he had possessed prior to his death. The *Rabbis* <note>original: "Rabbins"</note>, who were careful to cultivate this same knowledge after him, called this *Testament* “*The Clavicle, or Key of Solomon*.” <term>Clavicle</term> <note>From the Latin *clavicula*, meaning "little key." In this context, it refers to a "key" to unlocking hidden or magical knowledge.</note> They had this work engraved on pieces of tree bark, while the *Pentacles* <term>Pentacles</term> <note>Magical diagrams or talismans, often circular, used in ritual magic to command spirits or provide protection.</note> were inscribed in Hebrew letters on plates of copper, so that they might be carefully preserved in the *Temple* which that wise king had built.

In ancient times, this *Testament* was translated from *Hebrew* into the *Latin* language by *Rabbi Abognazar*, who took it with him to the town of *Arles* in *Provence*. There, by a remarkable stroke of good fortune, the ancient *Hebrew Clavicle*—that is to say, this precious translation of it—fell into the hands of the *Archbishop of Arles* after the destruction of the *Jews* in that city. The Archbishop translated it from *Latin* into the common language <note>original: "the vulgar tongue"</note> using the same terms which follow here, without having either changed or added anything to the original translation from the *Hebrew*.

<note>* Today, an angel is known as a good, helpful spirit on the Astral Plane. In Solomon’s time, they were called angels or devils. Today, they are spoken of as good or evil spirits or influences.—EDITOR’S NOTE.</note>

# The Key of Solomon.

->(The Little Key of Solomon <note>original: "CLAVICULA SALOMONIS"</note>)<-

---

## The Beginning of Book One.

### CHAPTER I.

->CONCERNING THE DIVINE LOVE WHICH PRECEDES THE ACQUISITION OF THIS KNOWLEDGE.<-

SOLOMON, THE SON OF DAVID, KING OF ISRAEL, has said that the beginning of our Key is to fear God <note>In this context, "fear" implies a sense of profound awe and religious reverence rather than terror.</note>, to adore Him, to honor Him with a repentant heart, to call upon Him* in all matters which we wish to undertake, and to operate with very great devotion; for in this way, God will lead us on the right path. Therefore, when you wish to acquire the knowledge of Magical Arts and Sciences, it is necessary to have prepared the timing of hours and of days, and of the position of the Moon. Without the use of these, you can achieve nothing; but if you observe them with diligence, you may easily and thoroughly reach the result and the end which you desire to attain.

---

<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

# BOOK ONE.

## CHAPTER II.

### CONCERNING THE DAYS, THE HOURS, AND THE POWERS <note>original: "Virtues"</note> OF THE PLANETS.

When* you wish to perform any experiment or ritual operation, you must first prepare all the necessary requirements beforehand, such as candles and incense†—which you will find described in the following chapters. You must also observe the days, the hours, and the other effects of the astrological alignments <note>original: "Constellations"</note> that are detailed in this chapter.

It is, therefore, useful to know that the hours of the day and night together total twenty-four, and that each hour is governed by one of the Seven Planets in a regular sequence, beginning with the highest and descending to the lowest. The order of the Planets is as follows: *Shabbathai* <note>Hebrew: SHBThAI, "The Restful One"</note>, Saturn; beneath Saturn is *Tzedeq* <note>Hebrew: TzDQ, "Justice"</note>, Jupiter; beneath Jupiter is *Madim* <note>Hebrew: MADIM, "The Red One"</note>, Mars; beneath Mars is *Shemesh* <note>Hebrew: ShMSH, "The Sun"</note>, the Sun; beneath the Sun is *Nogah* <note>Hebrew: NVGH, "Splendor"</note>, Venus; beneath Venus is *Kokav* <note>Hebrew: KVKB, "The Star"</note>, Mercury; and beneath Mercury is *Levanah* <note>Hebrew: LBNH, "The White One"</note>, the Moon, which is the lowest of all the Planets.

You must also understand that the Planets have dominion over the day that most closely matches the name assigned to them. For example: Saturday belongs to Saturn; Thursday to Jupiter; Tuesday to Mars; Sunday to the Sun; Friday to Venus; Wednesday to Mercury; and Monday to the Moon.

The rule of the Planets over each hour begins at dawn with the rising of the Sun on the day named after that Planet. The Planet that follows it in the sequence then takes over the rule of the next hour. For example, on Saturday, Saturn rules the first hour, Jupiter the second, Mars the third, the Sun the fourth, Venus the fifth, Mercury the sixth, and the Moon the seventh. Then, Saturn returns to rule the eighth hour, followed by the others in their turn; the Planets always maintain this same relative order.

Note that every experiment or magical operation should be performed under the influence of the Planet—and usually during the specific hour—that relates to that operation. For example:

*During the days and hours of Saturn, you can perform experiments to summon souls from the Underworld <note>original: "Hades"</note>, but only those who have died a natural death. Similarly, on these days and hours, you can work to bring either good or bad fortune to buildings; to have familiar spirits attend to you in your sleep; and to cause success or failure in business, property, goods, and crops.*

---

\* This first paragraph is missing in the Sloane Manuscript 1307 and the Additional Manuscript 10862.
† Those who wish to obtain Temple Incense or Candles will find these items listed in the extensive occult book catalog of Messrs. de Laurence, Scott & Co. <note>This refers to the publisher of this specific 1914 edition, who often inserted advertisements for their own supplies into the text.</note>

...for fruits and similar things; for acquiring learning; for bringing destruction and death; and for sowing hatred and discord.

*The Days and Hours of Jupiter* are suitable for obtaining honors and acquiring riches; for forming friendships and preserving health; and for achieving everything that you can desire.

*In the Days and Hours of Mars*, you can perform experiments <note>original: "experiments," meaning magical operations or rituals</note> regarding war; for attaining military honor and acquiring courage; for overthrowing enemies; and furthermore for causing ruin, slaughter, cruelty, and discord; and for wounding and causing death.

*The Days and Hours of the Sun* are very good for perfecting rituals regarding worldly <note>original: "temporal"</note> wealth, hope, gain, fortune, divination, and the favor of princes; for dissolving hostile feelings and for making friends.

*The Days and Hours of Venus* are good for forming friendships; for kindness and love; for joyous and pleasant undertakings; and for traveling.

*The Days and Hours of Mercury* are good for operations involving eloquence and intelligence; promptness in business; science and divination; wonders, apparitions, and answers regarding the future. You can also work under this planet for matters of theft, writing, deceit, and trade <note>original: "merchandise"</note>.

*The Days and Hours of the Moon* are good for embassies, voyages, envoys, messages, and navigation; for reconciliation and love; and for the acquisition of goods by water.*

You should take care to punctually observe all the instructions contained in this chapter if you desire to succeed, seeing that the truth of Magical Science depends upon them.

*The Hours of Saturn, Mars, and the Moon* are all good for communicating and speaking with spirits; just as those of Mercury are for recovering stolen goods by means of spirits.

*The Hours of Mars* serve for summoning souls from the underworld <note>original: "Hades"</note>,† especially those slain in battle.

*The Hours of the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus* are adapted for preparing any operations of love, kindness, or invisibility, as is shown more fully later on, along with other similar things contained in our work.

*The Hours of Saturn and Mars*, as well as the days on which the Moon is in conjunction ‡ with them, or when she is in opposition or quartile aspect, are excellent for performing experiments of hatred, enmity, quarreling, and discord, and other operations of the same kind which are given later in this work.

*The Hours of Mercury* are good for undertaking experiments relating to games, raillery <note>original: "raillery," meaning good-humored teasing or joking</note>, jests, sports, and similar activities.

*The Hours of the Sun, of Jupiter, and of Venus*, particularly on the days which they rule, are good for all extraordinary, uncommon, and unknown operations.

*The Hours of the Moon* are proper for making trial of experiments relating to the recovery of stolen property, for obtaining nocturnal visions, for summoning spirits in sleep, and for preparing anything relating to water.

*The Hours of Venus* are furthermore useful for lots <note>original: "lots," referring to games of chance or divination by drawing lots</note>, poisons, all things of the nature of Venus, for preparing powders provocative of madness, and similar things.

But in order to thoroughly carry out the operations of this Art, you should perform them not only during the planetary hours but on the days of the planets as well. Doing so ensures the experiment will always succeed better, provided you observe the rules laid down later on; for if you omit a single condition, you will never arrive at the accomplishment of the Art.

For those matters which pertain to the Moon, such as the invocation of spirits, the *works of necromancy* <note>original: "Necromancy," the practice of communicating with the spirits of the dead</note>, and the recovery of stolen property, it is necessary that the Moon should be in a terrestrial sign, namely: Taurus, Virgo, or Capricorn.

For love, grace, and invisibility, the Moon should be in a fiery sign, namely: Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius.

For hatred, discord, and destruction, the Moon should be in a watery sign, namely: Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces.

For experiments of a peculiar nature, which cannot be classified under any certain heading, the Moon should be in an airy sign, namely: Gemini, Libra, or Aquarius.

But if these things seem difficult for you to accomplish, it will suffice merely to notice the Moon after her combustion <note>original: "combustion," an astrological term for when a planet is so close to the Sun that its influence is weakened or hidden</note>, or conjunction with the Sun, especially just when she quits his beams and appears visible <note>i.e. the New Moon.</note>. For then it is good to perform all experiments for the construction and operation of any matter. That is why the time from the New until the Full Moon is proper for performing any of the experiments we have spoken of above. But during her decrease or wane, it is good for war, disturbance, and discord. Likewise, the period when she is almost deprived of light is proper for experiments of invisibility and of death.

But observe strictly that you commence nothing while the Moon is in conjunction with the Sun, seeing that this is extremely unfortunate and that you will then be able to achieve nothing. However, once the Moon quits the Sun's beams and increases in light, you can perform all that you desire, while nevertheless observing the directions in this chapter.

Furthermore, if you wish to converse with spirits it should be

Withdraw then to a secret place where no one can see you or interrupt you before the experiment is finished, whether you choose to work by day or by night. If you wish to work at night, finish your work on the following night; if by day—since the day begins at sunrise—finish your work on the following day. However, the hour to begin <note>original: "Hour of Inception"</note> must be the hour of Mercury.

Truly, because no experiments for communicating with spirits can be performed without preparing a circle, for any experiment you wish to undertake to speak with spirits, you must learn how to construct a specific circle. Once that is done, surround that circle with the "Circle of Art" <note>A protective boundary drawn on the ground intended to shield the practitioner from the spirits they summon.</note> for greater safety and effectiveness.

# BOOK ONE.

## CHAPTER III.

->CONCERNING THE ARTS.<-

If you wish to succeed, it is necessary to perform the following experiments <note>original: "Experiments"; in this context, these are ritual operations or magical workings.</note> and arts on the appropriate days and hours, with the required ceremonial acts and ceremonies contained and laid down in the following chapters.

Experiments, then, are of two kinds. The first is to test what, as I have said, can be easily performed without a Circle; in this case, it is not necessary to observe anything except what you will find in the relevant chapters. The second kind can in no way be brought to perfection without the Circle. In order to accomplish this perfectly, it is necessary to take note of all the preparations which the *Master* of the Art and his *Disciples* must undertake before constructing <note>The Sloane Manuscript 3091 says, “before they come to the Circle.”</note> the Circle.

Before beginning operations, both the *Master* and his *Disciples* must completely abstain for nine days from sensual pleasures and from vain and foolish conversation, as is plainly shown in the *Second Book, Chapter 4*. After six of these days have passed, he must frequently recite the Prayer and Confession as he will be instructed. On the seventh day, the Master being alone, let him enter into a secret place, take off his clothes, and bathe himself from head to foot in consecrated and exorcised water <note>Water that has been spiritually cleansed and blessed through a formal ritual.</note>, saying devoutly and humbly the prayer, “O Lord Adonai <note>A Hebrew name for God meaning "My Lord."</note>,” etc., as it is written in the *Second Book, Chapter 2*.

The prayer being finished, let the Master leave the water and put on his body clothing of white linen, clean and unsoiled. Then, let him go with his Disciples to a secret place and command them to strip themselves naked. Once they have taken off their clothes, let him take exorcised water and pour it over their heads so that it flows down to their feet and bathes them completely. While pouring this water over them, let the Master say: “*Be reborn, renewed, washed, and pure,*” *etc., as in Book II, Chapter 3*.

After this is done <note>This paragraph is omitted in the Lansdowne Manuscript 1202.</note>, the Disciples must dress themselves, putting on their bodies—like their Master—clothing of white linen, clean and unsoiled. For the last three days, the Master and his Disciples should fast, observing the ceremonial acts and prayers marked in *Book II, Chapter 2*.

Note that during the last three days the weather should be calm, without wind and without clouds moving quickly across the face of the sky. On the last day, let the Master go with his Disciples to a secret fountain of running water, or to a flowing stream, and there let each of them, taking off his clothes, wash himself with due ceremony, as is described
