# The Key of Solomon

## The Beginning of Book Two

### BOOK TWO

### CHAPTER I

### AT WHAT HOUR AFTER PREPARING EVERYTHING NECESSARY, WE SHOULD BRING THE PRACTICE OF THE ART TO PERFECTION.

The days and hours have already been discussed in general terms in the First Book. It is now necessary to note specifically at what hour you should complete and perfect your rituals, once all the necessary preparations have been made.

If you have undertaken any secret operation to converse with or conjure spirits in which the day and hour are not specified, you shall perform it on the days and hours of Mercury, at the sixteenth or twenty-third hour. However, it will be even better at the eighth hour, which is the third hour of the night (meaning before dawn); then you will be able to perform all the arts and operations you wish, by day or by night, provided they were prepared at their appropriate hours, as has been said before. But when no specific hour or time for the operation or invocation is mentioned, it is much better to perform these experiments at night, since it is easier for spirits to appear in the peaceful silence of the night than during the day. You should strictly observe that if you wish to invoke spirits, whether by day or by night, it must be done in a hidden, remote, secret, and convenient place suitable for such an art, where no one else visits or lives, as we will explain more fully in the appropriate section.

If you are performing an operation regarding anything that has been stolen, however it is performed or prepared, it is necessary to practice it on the days and hours of the Moon. If possible, this should be while she is increasing <note>the waxing moon</note>, and between the first and eighth hours of the day.

But if it is done at night, then it should be at the fifth or the third hour; however, it is better by day than by night, for the light justifies <note>original: "justifieth"; here meaning it makes the results clear or manifest</note> them and makes them much more suitable for revelation <note>original: "publication"; the act of making the theft or thief known</note>. But if the operations concern invisibility, they should be practiced at the first, second, and third hours of Mars by day. But if at night, then until the third hour. If they are operations for seeking love, grace, or favor, they should be performed until the eighth hour of that same day, starting from the...

<summary>This page outlines the specific astrological timing and environmental conditions necessary for successful magical operations, emphasizing the roles of Mercury, the Moon, and Mars.</summary>

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON.

first hour of the Sun; and from the first hour of Venus until the first hour of the same day of Venus.

As for operations of destruction and desolation, we should practice and perform them on the day of Saturn at the first hour, or preferably at the eighth or fifteenth hour of the day; and from the first until the eighth hour of the night.

Experiments regarding games, mockery <note>original: "raillery"</note>, deceit, illusion, and invisibility should be done at the first hour of Venus and at the eighth hour of the day; but by night at the third and seventh hours.

At all times when practicing and performing the Magical Arts, the Moon should be increasing in light <note>waxing</note>, and in an equal number of degrees with the Sun; and it is much better from the first quarter to the full moon <note>original: "Opposition"</note>, and the Moon should be in a fiery zodiac sign, notably in that of Aries <note>original: "the Ram"</note> or Leo <note>original: "the Lion"</note>.

Therefore, to perform these experiments in any manner whatsoever, they should be done when the Moon is clear and when she is waxing.

In order to perform those of invisibility after everything is properly prepared, the Moon should be in the sign of Pisces <note>original: "the Fishes"</note>, during the proper and fitting hours, and she should be waxing.

For experiments seeking love and favor, in whatever way it may be desired, they will succeed provided that they have been prepared at the proper hours, and that the Moon is waxing and in the sign of Gemini <note>original: "the Twins"</note>.

Such exact preparation of days and hours is not necessary for those who are <term>Adepts</term> <note>experts or masters of the craft</note> in the Art, but it is extremely necessary for apprentices and beginners. This is because those who have been little or not at all instructed in this, and who are only beginning to apply themselves to this Art, do not have as much faith in the experiments as those who are experts and have practiced them. But as for beginners, they should always ensure the days and hours are well-chosen and appropriate for the Art. The Wise should only observe the necessary rules <term>precepts</term> of the Art, and by observing the other required ceremonies <term>solemnities</term>, they will operate with perfect confidence <note>original: "assurance"</note>.

It is, nevertheless, necessary to take care that when you have prepared any experiment yourself for the appointed days and hours, it should be performed in clear, calm <note>original: "serene"</note>, mild, and pleasant weather, without any great storm <note>original: "tempest"</note> or disturbance of the air, which should not be troubled by winds. For when you conjure any spirits in any art or experiment, they will not appear when the air is troubled or agitated by winds, because spirits have neither flesh nor bones and are created of different substances.

->Some are created from water.<-
->Others from wind, which they resemble.<-
->Some from earth.<-
->Some from clouds.<-

--- Page 112 ---
<meta>This page continues the classification of spirits based on their elemental origins and celestial natures, describing how they appear and behave when summoned. It follows a discussion on the importance of timing and planetary hours in magical practice.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON.

Others originate from solar vapors.

Others come from the intensity and strength of fire; and when they are invoked or summoned, they always arrive with a great noise and with the terrifying nature of fire.

When the spirits which are created from water are invoked, they arrive with heavy rains, thunder, hail, lightning, thunderbolts, and similar phenomena.

When the spirits which are created from clouds are invoked, they appear in a very deformed and horrible shape to strike fear into the summoner <note>original: "invocator"</note>, accompanied by an extremely loud noise.

Others* which are formed from wind appear like the wind itself and move with extreme speed. Whenever those created from beauty† appear, they will show themselves in a lovely and pleasant form. Furthermore, whenever you call the spirits created from air, they will arrive as a kind of gentle breeze.

When the spirits which are created from the vapors of the sun are invoked, they appear in a very beautiful and excellent form, but they are full of pride, vanity, and conceit. They are clever; for this reason, Solomon specifies all of these in his book of ornament or beauty. They display great ostentation and vanity in their clothing and delight in many ornaments; they boast of possessing worldly <note>original: "mundane"</note> beauty and all kinds of decorations. You should only invoke them in clear, mild, and pleasant weather.

The spirits created from fire reside in the east, while those created from wind reside in the south.

Note, then, that it is much better to perform experiments or operations facing east, putting everything necessary into practice toward that direction.

However, for all other operations, extraordinary experiments, and those regarding love, they will be much more effective if directed toward the north.

Take further care that every time you perform an experiment to bring it to perfection with the required ceremonies <note>original: "solemnities"</note>, you must restart the previous experiment if you are interrupted, without needing to re-prepare the hours or other ceremonies.

If by chance it happens that after performing an experiment while observing the proper days, hours, and required ceremonies, you find it unsuccessful, then it must be somehow false, poorly arranged, or defective. You have certainly failed in some detail; for if you do even one thing wrong, these experiments and arts will not be proven true <note>original: "verified"</note>.

Thus, this whole *Key of Arts, Experiments, and Operations* depends upon this chapter. Even if every ceremony is correctly observed, no experiment will succeed <note>original: "be verified"</note> unless you can understand the deep meaning of this chapter.

***

\* This paragraph is only found in 10862 Add. MSS. <note>Refers to a specific manuscript in the British Library collection.</note>
† The name of the sixth Qabalistic Sephira, or emanation from the Deity, which is called *Tiphereth*, or Beauty. <note>In the Qabalah, Tiphereth represents balance, beauty, and the Sun.</note>

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON.

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER II.

### HOW THE MASTER OF THE ART SHOULD CONDUCT, RULE, AND GOVERN HIMSELF.

Anyone who wishes to apply themselves to such a great and difficult science should keep their mind free from all business and from any distracting thoughts of any kind.

He should then thoroughly examine the ritual <note>original: "Art or Operation"</note> he intends to undertake. He should write it out clearly on paper specifically set aside for that purpose, including the appropriate conjurations and exorcisms. If anything needs to be marked or written down, it should be done using the specific paper, ink, and pen described later. He should also observe the specific day and hour this experiment should be started, what preparations are necessary, what should be added, and what can be left out.

Once these matters are prepared, you must find and arrange a suitable place where the magical arts and experiments can be practiced. When everything is ready, the Master of the Art should go to a proper location, such as his private study <note>original: "Cabinet or Secret Chamber"</note> if it is suitable, to set the entire operation in order. Alternatively, he can use any other convenient, secret place, provided that no one knows where it is and no one can see him while he is there.

After this, he must undress completely and prepare a bath of exorcised water <term>exorcised water: water that has been ritually purified and blessed to drive out any spiritual impurities</term>, following the method we will describe. He should bathe and purify himself from the crown of his head to the soles of his feet, saying:

<margin>†</margin>
> O Lord ADONAI <note>original: "ADONAI", a Hebrew name for God meaning "My Lord"</note>, Who has formed me, Your unworthy servant, in Your image and likeness out of vile and lowly earth; deign to bless and sanctify this water, so that it may serve for the health and purification of my soul and body, and that no foolishness or deceit may find any place within it.

> O Most Powerful and Inexpressible <note>original: "Ineffable"</note> God, Who made Your people pass dry-shod through the *Red Sea* when they came out of the Land of *Egypt*, grant me the grace to be purified and reborn from all my past sins by this water, so that no uncleanness may appear upon me in Your presence.

After this, you shall immerse yourself completely in the water and dry yourself with a towel of clean white linen. Then you shall put

...you shall put upon your body the garments of pure white linen, which we will describe later.

After this, for at least three days, you shall abstain from all idle, shallow, and impure thoughts, and from every kind of impurity and sin, as will be shown in the chapter regarding fasting and vigils <term>vigil: a period of staying awake during the night for spiritual observation or prayer</term>. Each day you shall recite the following prayer: at least once in the morning, twice around noon, three times in the afternoon, four times in the evening, and five times before going to sleep. You shall do this for the three following days:

->**THE PRAYER**<-

HERACHIO, ASAC, ASACRO, BEDRIMULAEL, TILATH, ARABONAS, IERAHLEM, IDEODOC, ARCHARZEL, ZOPHIEL, BLAUTEL, BARACATA, EDONIEL, ELOHIM <note>Hebrew: God</note>, EMAGRO, ABRAGATEH, SAMOEL, GEBURAHEL, CADATO, ERA, ELOHI, ACHSAH, EBMISHA, IMACHEDEL, DANIEL, DAMA, ELAMOS, IZACHEL, BAEL, SEGON, GEMON, DEMAS.

O Lord God, who are seated above the Heavens and who look down upon the depths <term>Abysses: the deep, hidden places of the earth or the spiritual realm</term> below, grant me Your grace, I beseech You. Let that which I conceive in my mind be accomplished in my work, through You, O God, the Sovereign Ruler of all, who live and reign forever and ever <note>original: "unto the Ages of the Ages"</note>. Amen.

Once these three days have passed, you must have everything ready, as has been described, and then set aside a specific day for the work. You must wait for the exact hour in which you should begin the operation <note>original: "Operation," referring to the specific magical ritual or experiment</note>. However, once it has begun at the correct hour, you will be able to continue it until the end. This is because the ritual derives its force and power from its beginning, which extends and spreads over the following hours. In this way, the Master of the Art <note>the practitioner or magician</note> will be able to complete his work and achieve the desired result.

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON.

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER III.

### HOW THE COMPANIONS OR DISCIPLES OF THE MASTER OF THE ART OUGHT TO REGULATE AND GOVERN THEMSELVES.

When the Master of the Art <term>Master of the Art: the lead practitioner or magician conducting the ritual</term> wishes to put into practice any operation or experiment, especially an important one, he should first consider which companions he should use. This is why, in every operation that should be carried out within the Circle, it is best to have three companions. If he cannot find companions, he should at least have a faithful and devoted dog with him. But if it is absolutely necessary for him to have companions, these companions must be obligated and bound by an oath to do everything the Master orders or prescribes for them. They should study, observe, carefully remember, and pay close attention to everything they hear. For those who act otherwise will suffer and endure many pains and hardships and run into many dangers brought upon them by the spirits; because of this, they may even die.

Once the disciples are thoroughly instructed and strengthened with a wise and understanding heart, the Master shall take exorcised water <term>exorcised water: water that has been spiritually cleansed through a specific ritual or prayer</term> and enter a secret, purified, and clean place with them. There, he must have them strip entirely naked. After this, he should pour the exorcised water over their heads, letting it flow from the crown of the head to the soles of the feet to bathe them completely. While bathing them this way, he should say:

> Be regenerated, cleansed, and purified from all your sins in the name of the Ineffable <term>Ineffable: too great or sacred to be expressed in words</term>, Great, and Eternal God. May the virtue of the Most High descend upon you and remain with you always, so that you may have the power and strength to accomplish the desires of your heart. Amen.

After this, the disciples should dress themselves just as the Master has done and fast for three days as he did, repeating the same prayer. They should act like him and follow and obey him implicitly in all things during the work.

But if the Master of the Art wishes to have a dog as his companion, he must bathe the dog thoroughly with exorcised water in the same way as the disciples. He should then perfume the dog with the scents and incense of the Art <term>incense of Art: specific herbal blends or perfumes used in magical rituals</term> and recite the following conjuration <term>conjuration: a formal appeal or ritual command to a spirit or entity</term> over him:

> I conjure you, O creature, being a dog, by Him who has

It also provides an alternative for practitioners working alone: the ritual preparation of a dog to serve as a companion.</summary>

...created you, I bathe and I perfume you in the Name of the Most High, Most Powerful, and Eternal God, so that you may be my true companion in this operation, and that you may also be my faithful friend in whatever operation I may perform hereafter.

But if he wishes to have for his companion a little boy or girl—which is even better—he must prepare them as he has prepared the dog. He must trim and cut the nails of their hands and of their feet, saying:

> I conjure you, O creature, being a young girl (or boy), by the Most High God, the Father of all creatures, by the Father Lord God <note>original: "ADONAI ELOHIM"</note>, and by the Father Most High <note>original: "ELION"</note>, that you shall have neither the will nor the power to hide anything from me, nor yet to withhold the truth in all that I shall demand of you, and that you be obedient and faithful to me. Amen.

Let him purify, cleanse, and wash this young child again with the <term>Water of Art</term> <note>Water of Art: water that has been ritually exorcised and consecrated for use in magic</note>, saying:

> Be regenerate, cleansed, and purified, so that the spirits may neither harm you nor remain within you. Amen.

Then perfume the child with incense as described above.

When the companions are thus ordained and prepared, the Master shall be able to work in safety together with them whenever he wishes; and he shall perform his operation successfully and attain his goal.

But for the safety of both soul and body, the Master and the companions should wear <term>Pentacles</term> <note>Pentacles: ceremonial symbols or talismans, often drawn on parchment or engraved on metal, used to provide protection and authority over spirits</note> over their chests. These must be consecrated, covered with a silk veil, and perfumed with the proper <term>fumigations</term> <note>fumigations: the ritual burning of specific herbs or incense to create a sacred atmosphere and attract or repel spirits</note>. Being assured and encouraged by these, they may enter into the work without fear or terror, and they shall be exempt and free from all perils and dangers, provided that they obey the commands of the Master and do all that he orders them. If they act in this way, all things shall go according to their desires.

All being thus arranged, the Master should ensure that his disciples are perfectly instructed in those things which they have to perform.

These companions or disciples should be three in number, not including the Master. They may also be five, seven, or nine in number; but they must always implicitly obey the orders of their Master; for only in this way shall all things reach a successful conclusion.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER IV.

### CONCERNING THE FASTING, CARE, AND THINGS TO BE OBSERVED.

When the Master of the Art wishes to perform his operations, having previously arranged everything that is necessary to observe and practice, he must—from the very first day of the <term>Experiment</term> <note>original: "Experiment"; in this context, it refers to the specific magical ritual or operation being undertaken</note>—absolutely establish and follow a regime of care and observation. He must abstain from all unlawful things and from every kind of impiety, impurity, wickedness, or immodesty of both body and soul. For example, he should avoid eating and drinking excessively, and refrain from all sorts of vain words, foolishness, slanders, insults, and other useless talk. 

Instead, he should perform good deeds, speak honestly, and maintain strict decency in all things. He must never lose sight of modesty in his walking, conversation, eating, drinking, and in all other actions. This discipline should be primarily practiced and observed for nine days before the commencement of the <term>Operation</term>. The disciples should do the same and must equally put into practice everything necessary to be observed if they wish to make use of these operations and experiments.

However, before the work begins, it is absolutely necessary for the Master and his disciples to repeat the following conjuration once in the morning and twice in the evening:

->THE CONJURATION.<-

> O Lord God Almighty, be favorable to me, a miserable sinner, for I am not worthy to raise my eyes toward heaven because of the iniquity of my sins and the multitude of my faults. O pitying and merciful Father, who does not desire the death of a sinner but rather that he should turn from his wickedness and live <note>This is a reference to the biblical passage Ezekiel 33:11</note>; O God, have mercy on me and pardon all my sins. 
>
> Though I am unworthy, I entreat You, O Father of all Creatures, You who are full of mercy and compassion, by Your great goodness: deign to grant me the power to see and know these spirits which I desire to behold, and to invoke them to appear before me and accomplish my will. I ask this through You, who are the Conqueror and who are blessed throughout the <term>Ages of the Ages</term> <note>original: "Ages of the Ages"; a translation of the Latin "In saecula saeculorum," meaning forever and ever</note>. Amen.
>
> O Lord God, the Eternal Father, who is seated upon the <term>Cherubim</term> and the <term>Seraphim</term> <note>The Cherubim and Seraphim are the highest orders of angels in traditional celestial hierarchy</note>, who looks upon the earth and the sea; to You I raise my hands and implore Your aid alone. You are the only one who brings good works to completion; You give rest to those who labor, humble the proud, and are the Author of Life and the Destroyer of Death. You are our rest; You are the Protector of those...

...who invoke You; protect, guard, and defend me in this matter and in this enterprise which I propose to carry out, O You Who live, reign, and remain through the eternal ages. Amen.

During the last three days before beginning this action, you shall be content with eating only fasting food, and that only once a day; it will be even better if you eat only bread and water. You must also abstain from every impure thing, while reciting the prayer written above. On the last day, when you wish to begin the <term>Operation</term> <note>original: "Operation"; a ritual or magical act</note>, you must go the whole day without eating. Later, you shall go to a secret place where you will confess all your sins to God with a repentant heart. The disciples and the Master shall also recite the same confession in a low but clear voice, as was already described in the First Book.

Once this has been done three times with a devoted, pure, and repentant heart in a secluded, clean, and pure place where you cannot be seen, you shall take the water and the <term>hyssop</term> <note>original: "hyssop"; a plant used historically for ritual cleansing and purification</note> and say:

> Purify me, O Lord, with hyssop, and I shall be pure; wash me and I shall be whiter than snow. <note>original: "Purifica me Domine hyssopo..."; a reference to Psalm 51:7 used in the Asperges rite of purification.</note>

After this, bathe yourself with the <term>exorcised water</term> <note>water that has been ritually purified and blessed</note> and put on the <term>consecrated garment</term> <note>a specific robe or outfit dedicated to ritual use</note> that you had taken off. Cense yourself and surround yourself with fragrances, as will be explained later when we discuss perfumes and <term>suffumigations</term> <note>original: "suffumigations"; the act of using smoke or fumes, such as incense, for ritual purification</note>.

When this is done, you shall go to the designated place with your companions. Once everything is prepared, you shall draw the <term>Circle</term> <note>the ritual space, often a circle drawn on the ground to protect the magician</note>, as previously described, along with all other necessary ceremonies. Then you shall begin to call upon the spirits using the <term>Exorcisms</term> <note>ritual commands or prayers used to summon or control spirits</note>. You must also repeat the confession mentioned earlier, as described in the First Book. Afterward, as a sign of change and repentance, everyone shall kiss one another.

Note well that up to this point, the disciples should perform the same actions as the Master.

Let the Master now give his commands to his disciples and continue with the <term>Experiment</term> <note>original: "Experiment"; in this context, the specific magical ritual being performed</note>, working with all diligence to bring it to completion.

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER V.

#### CONCERNING THE BATHS, AND HOW THEY ARE TO BE ARRANGED.

THE Bath is necessary for all *Magical and Necromantic Arts* <note>original: "Necromantic Arts"; in this context, it refers generally to spirit magic or communication with the beyond, rather than just the modern definition of talking to the dead.</note>; therefore, if you wish to perform any experiment or operation, having arranged all things necessary for it according to the proper days and hours, you shall go to a river or running stream, or you shall have warm water ready in some large vessel or tub in your secret cabinet. While undressing yourself of your clothing, you shall repeat the following Psalms: Psalms 14 (or 53), 27, 54, 81, and 105. <note>The author provides two numbers for the first Psalm because numbering differs between the Hebrew and Latin Bibles.</note>

And when the Master is entirely undressed, let him enter into the water or into the Bath, and let him say:

->THE EXORCISM OF THE WATER.<-

I exorcise you, O Creature of Water <note>original: "Creature of Water"; in historical magic, "creature" refers to any created thing or element</note>, by Him Who has created you and gathered you together into one place so that the dry land appeared, that you uncover all the deceits of the Enemy, and that you cast out from yourself all the impurities and uncleanness of the Spirits of the World of Phantasm <note>The realm of illusions or malevolent spirits</note>, so they may not harm me, through the virtue of God Almighty who lives and reigns through all the ages. Amen. <note>original: "unto the Ages of the Ages"; a translation of the Latin "in saecula saeculorum," meaning forever.</note>

Then you shall begin to wash yourself thoroughly in the Bath, saying:

MERTALIA, MUSALIA, DOPHALIA, ONEMALIA, ZITANSEIA, GOLDA-
PHAIRA, DEDULSAIRA, GHEVIALAIRA, GHEMINAIRA, GEGROPHEIRA,
CEDAHI, GILTHAR, GODIEB, EZOIIL, MUSIL, GRASSIL, TAMEN, PUERI,
GODU, HUZNOTH, ASTACHOTH, TZABAOTH, ADONAI, AGLA, ON, EL,
TETRAGRAMMATON, SHEMA, ARESION, ANAPHAXETON, SEGILATON,
PRIMEUMATON. <note>These are "Words of Power" or "Barbarous Names." They are often corruptions of Hebrew or Greek divine titles, used for their perceived vibrational and ritual potency.</note>

All of these Names you shall repeat twice or thrice, until you are completely washed and clean. When you are perfectly pure, you shall leave the Bath and sprinkle yourself with exorcised water, in the manner described later on, and you shall say:

> Purge me, O Lord, with hyssop <note>original: "hyssop"; a plant used for ritual purification</note>, and I shall be clean; wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow. <note>A recitation of Psalm 51:7.</note>

While dressing yourself again, you shall recite the following Psalms: Psalms 102, 51, 4, 30, 119 (specifically the section *Mem*, verse 97), 114, 126, and 139.

After which you shall recite the following prayer:

->PRAYER.<-

EL <note>A Hebrew name for God</note>, Strong and Wonderful, I bless You, I adore You, I glorify You, I invoke You, I render You thanks from this Bath, so that this...

May this water be able to cast away from me all impurity and lust <note>original: "concupiscence"; refers to strong desire or sensual longing</note> of heart through You, O Holy Adonai; and may I accomplish all things through You, Who lives and reigns forever and ever <note>original: "unto the Ages of the Ages"; a literal translation of the Latin liturgical phrase *in saecula saeculorum*</note>. Amen.

After this, take the Salt and bless it in this manner:

### THE BLESSING OF THE SALT.

The Blessing of the Father Almighty be upon this Creature of Salt <note>In historical magic, "creature" refers to any created thing or element of nature</note>, and let all malice and hindrance be cast out from it, and let all good enter into it. For without You, man cannot live; therefore I bless you and invoke you, so that you may aid me.

Then you shall recite Psalm 103 over the Salt.

Then, taking the grains of the ritually cleansed <note>original: "exorcised"</note> Salt, you shall cast them into the previously mentioned Bath; and you shall again undress yourself, pronouncing the following words:

->IMANEL, ARNAMON, IMATO, MEMEON, RECTACON, MUOBOII, PALTELLON, DECAION, YAMENTON, YARON, TATONON, VAPHORON, GARDON, EXISTON, ZAGVERON, MOMERTON, ZARMESITON, TILEION, TIXMION.<-
<note>These are "Barbarous Names of Evocation," strings of words from various or unknown languages believed to hold power through their sound and antiquity.</note>

After this, you shall enter the Bath a second time and recite Psalms 104 and 81.

Then you shall leave the Bath and clothe yourself as before in clean, white linen garments. Over them, you shall put the special garments which we shall describe in the proper Chapter; and once clothed, you shall go to finish your work.

The Disciples should wash themselves in the same way and with the same ceremonies <note>original: "solemnities"</note>.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER VI.

### CONCERNING THE GARMENTS AND SHOES OF THE ART.

The outer clothing <note>original: "habiliments"</note> which the Master of the Art should wear should be made of linen, as should the garments he wears beneath them; if he has the financial means, they should ideally be made of silk. If they are made of linen, the thread used to make them should have been spun by a young maiden.

The symbols <note>original: "characters"</note> shown in *Figure 55* should be embroidered on the breast with the ritual needle <note>original: "needle of Art"</note> using red silk.

The shoes should also be white, upon which the symbols in *Figure 56* should be embroidered in the same manner.

The shoes or boots should be made of white leather, and the signs and symbols of the Art should be marked upon them. These shoes should be crafted during days of fasting and abstinence—specifically, during the nine days set aside before the beginning of the ritual operation. During this time, the necessary tools should also be prepared, polished, brightened, and cleaned.

In addition to this, the Master of the Art should have a crown made of virgin parchment <note>original: "Virgin Parchment paper"; traditionally parchment made from the skin of an animal that had not mated, signifying ritual purity</note>, upon which these four Names should be written: **YOD, HE, VAU, HE** <note>The four Hebrew letters of the Tetragrammaton, the sacred name of God</note> in the front; **ADONAI** <note>Hebrew: "Lord"</note> behind; **EL** <note>Hebrew: "God"</note> on the right; and **ELOHIM** <note>Hebrew: "God" or "Powers"</note> on the left (*See Figure 57*). These names should be written with the ritual ink and pen, which we will discuss in the appropriate chapter. Each of the disciples should also have a crown of virgin parchment where these Divine symbols are marked in scarlet (*See Figure 58*).

Take care that while you are putting on these garments, you recite these Psalms: **Psalms 15, 131, 137, 117, 67, 68, and 127.**

After this, perfume the garments <note>original: "Vestments"</note> by burning Temple Incense, and sprinkle them with the ritual water and hyssop <note>Hyssop is a plant used since biblical times for ritual purification and sprinkling holy water</note>.

When the Master and his disciples begin to dress themselves after the first Psalm, and before continuing with the others, he should pronounce these words:

> **LOVE, LOVER** <note>original: "AMOR, AMATOR"; Latin</note>, **AMIDES, IDEODANIACH, PAMOR, PLAIOR, ANITOR** <note>These are "Barbarous Names," words of power often derived from ancient languages whose exact meanings are secondary to their sound and tradition</note>; through the merits of these holy Angels, I will dress and clothe myself with the Garments of Power, through which I may bring to a desired conclusion those things which I intensely desire, through You, O Most Holy **LORD** <note>original: "ADONAI"</note>, Whose Kingdom and Empire endures forever. Amen.

Take notice that if the linen garments were originally the vestments of Levites or priests, and had been used for holy services, they would be even more effective.

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER VII.

### CONCERNING THE PLACES WHERE WE MAY CONVENIENTLY PERFORM THE EXPERIMENTS AND OPERATIONS OF THE ART.

The places best suited for performing and completing magical arts and operations are those that are concealed, remote, and separated from human dwellings. Therefore, desolate and uninhabited regions are most appropriate, such as the shores of lakes, forests, dark and obscure places, old and deserted houses where people rarely or hardly ever come, mountains, caves, caverns, grottos, gardens, and orchards. However, the best places of all are crossroads—specifically where four roads meet—during the depth and silence of the night. But if you cannot conveniently go to any of these places, your house, your own chamber, or indeed any place will be found fit and convenient for the summoning and assembling of the spirits, provided it has been purified and consecrated with the necessary ceremonies.

These arts or operations should be carried out at the prescribed time, but if no specific time is appointed, it will always be better to perform them at night. This is the most suitable and proper time for the operations of necromancy <note>original: "Necromancy"; here referring generally to the conjuration of spirits</note>; it is also a symbol that it is just and right to hide these acts from the sight of the foolish, the ignorant, and the uninitiated <note>original: "profane"</note>.

Once you have selected a fitting place, you may perform your experiments by day or by night. The area should be spacious, clear, and bounded on all sides by hedges, shrubs, trees, or walls. You shall clean it thoroughly yourself and make it neat and pure, and while doing this, you shall recite Psalms 2, 67, and 54.

After this, you shall perfume the area with the scents and ritual incense <note>original: "suffumigations"; the use of smoke from burning substances for ritual purification</note> of the Art, and sprinkle it with the water and the hyssop <note>A plant used for ritual sprinkling and purification in biblical and occult traditions</note>. After doing this, you may make all the necessary preparations for an operation in this place.

But when you later go to this place to complete and accomplish the operation, you shall repeat the following prayer in a low and distinct voice on the way there:

->THE PRAYER.<-

> **ZAZAII, ZAMAII, PUIDAMON** Most Powerful, **SEDON** Most Strong, **EL** <note>Hebrew: "God"</note>, **YOD HE VAU HE** <note>The Tetragrammaton, the sacred four-letter name of God</note>, **IAH** <note>A shortened form of the divine name</note>, **AGLA** <note>A kabbalistic acronym for the Hebrew phrase "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai," meaning "Thou art mighty forever, O Lord"</note>, assist me, an unworthy sinner, who has had the boldness to pronounce these Holy Names which no man should name or invoke except in very great danger. Therefore, I have recourse...

<summary>This chapter provides instructions for selecting a ritual site, emphasizing secluded locations or crossroads and the necessary steps for purifying the space through prayer, incense, and holy water.</summary>
<keywords>ritual location, crossroads, necromancy, purification, Psalms, incense, hyssop, divine names, Tetragrammaton</keywords>

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON.

...to these Most Holy Names, being in great danger of both soul and body. Forgive me if I have sinned in any way, for I trust in Your protection alone, especially on this journey.

As the Master walks, he should sprinkle the path with the water and hyssop of the Art, while each of his disciples repeats in a low voice the prayer we have prescribed for the days of fasting and preparation.

Furthermore, the Master should appoint his disciples to carry the items necessary for the Art.

The first shall carry the censer, the fire, and the incense.

The second: the book, the paper, the pens, the ink, and the various perfumes.

The third: the knife and the sickle.

The Master: the staff and the wand.

If there are more disciples present, the Master shall distribute the items for each to carry according to their number.

When they have arrived at the location and everything is arranged in its proper order, the Master shall take the knife or another suitable consecrated <term>magical steel tool</term> to draw the Circle of Art he intends to create. Once this is done, he must perfume the circle with incense and sprinkle it with water. After warning and encouraging his disciples, he shall work as follows:

First, he should have a trumpet made of new wood. On one side, the divine names **ELOHIM GIBOR** <note>original: "ELOHIM GIBOR"; Hebrew for "God of Might"</note> and **ELOHIM TZABAOTH** <note>original: "ELOHIM TZABAOTH"; Hebrew for "God of Hosts"</note> should be written in Hebrew using the pen and ink of the Art (*see Figure 59*). On the other side, these characters should be written (*see Figure 60*).

After entering the circle to perform the experiment, he should sound his trumpet toward the four quarters of the universe: first toward the East, then the South, then the West, and finally the North. Then he shall say:

> Hear me and be ready—in whatever part of the universe you may be—to obey the voice of God the Mighty One and the names of the Creator. We notify you by this signal and sound that you will be summoned here; therefore, hold yourselves in readiness to obey our commands.

When this is finished, the Master shall complete his work, renew the circle, and perform the incense offerings and fumigations <note>original: "incensements and fumigations"; the ritual burning of herbs and resins to purify the space or attract spirits</note>.

<summary>This page describes the procession to the ritual site, the distribution of magical tools among disciples, and the initial consecration of the Magic Circle using a wooden trumpet and divine names.</summary>
<keywords>ritual procession, magic circle, Elohim Gibor, Elohim Tzabaoth, ritual trumpet, consecration, fumigation</keywords>

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER VIII.

### OF THE KNIFE, SWORD, SICKLE, PONIARD, DAGGER, LANCE, WAND, STAFF, AND OTHER INSTRUMENTS OF MAGICAL ART.

In order to properly carry out the greatest and most important operations of the Art, various instruments are necessary, such as a knife with a white handle, another with a black handle, and a short lance with which to trace circles, characters, and other things.

The knife with the white handle (*see Figure 61*) should be made on the day and hour of Mercury <note>The planetary hour system assigns specific times of the day to the seven traditional planets; Mercury is associated with intelligence and the craft of magic.</note>, when Mars is in the sign of Aries <note>original: "the Ram"</note> or of Scorpio <note>original: "the Scorpion"</note>. It should be dipped in the blood of a gosling <note>A young goose.</note> and in the juice of the pimpernel, while the moon is full or waxing <note>original: "increasing in light"</note>. Dip the white handle into this mixture as well, upon which you shall have engraved the characters shown. Afterwards, perfume it with the incense <note>original: "perfumes"</note> of the Art.

With this knife you may perform all the necessary operations of the Art, except for drawing the circles. But if it seems too difficult for you to make a knife in this way, you may have one made in the same shape. You shall then place it in the fire three times until it becomes red-hot, and each time you shall immerse it in the blood and juice mentioned before. Fasten the white handle to it, having engraved the specified characters upon it; and upon the handle you shall write these names—**Agla** <note>A Hebrew acronym for *Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai*, meaning "You, O Lord, are mighty forever."</note> and **On** <note>A divine name often used in medieval magic, sometimes associated with the sun or eternal being.</note>—with the pen of Art, starting from the point and moving toward the handle, as shown in *Figure 61*. Afterward, you shall perfume and sprinkle it <note>Ritual sprinkling with "exorcised water" or holy water.</note>, and wrap it in a piece of silk cloth.

As for the knife with the black handle (*see Figure 62*) used for making the circle and for striking terror and fear into the spirits, it should be made in the same manner, except that it should be done on the day and hour of Saturn <note>Saturn is associated with restriction, binding, and the underworld.</note>. It should be dipped in the blood of a black cat and in the juice of hemlock, with the characters and names shown in *Figure 62* written on it from the point toward the handle. When this is completed, you shall wrap it in a black silk cloth.

The scimitar (*Figure 63*) and the sickle (*Figure 64*) are made in the same way, as are the dagger (*Figure 65*), the poniard (*Figure 66*), and the short lance (*Figure 67*), on the day and hour of Mercury. They should be dipped in the blood of a magpie and the juice of the herb Mercury <note>A common plant, *Mercurialis annua*, used in folk medicine and magic.</note>. You must make handles for them out of white boxwood cut from the tree with a single stroke at sunrise, using a new knife or any other suitable tool. The characters shown should...

<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

...be traced upon them. You shall perfume them according to the rules of Art and wrap them in silk cloth like the others.

The Staff (*see Figure 68*) should be made of elderwood, cane, or rosewood; and the Wand (*Figure 69*) should be of hazel or nut tree. In all cases, the wood must be "virgin," meaning it is from a single year’s growth. Each should be cut from the tree with a single stroke on the day of Mercury <note>Wednesday</note> at sunrise. The characters shown should be written or engraved on them during the day and hour of Mercury.

When this is done, you shall say:

> <term>Adonai</term> <note>Hebrew: "Lord"</note>, Most Holy, deign to bless and to consecrate this Wand and this Staff, so that they may obtain the necessary power through You, O Most Holy Lord, whose kingdom endures forever and ever <note>original: "unto the Ages of the Ages"</note>. Amen.

After having perfumed and consecrated them, put them aside in a pure and clean place to be used when required.

Swords are also frequently necessary for use in the Magical Arts. You shall, therefore, take a new sword, which you shall clean and polish on the day of Mercury, at either the first or the fifteenth hour. After this, you shall write these Divine Names in Hebrew on one side: <term>Yod He Vau He</term> <note>The Tetragrammaton, or the four-letter name of God</note>, <term>Adonai</term> <note>Lord</note>, <term>Eheieh</term> <note>I Am</note>, and <term>Yayai</term> <note>A name for God often used in mystical contexts</note>. On the other side, write <term>Elohim Gibor</term> <note>God of Might</note> (*see Figure 70*). Sprinkle and cense it with incense, and repeat the following conjuration over it:

### THE CONJURATION OF THE SWORD.

> I conjure you, O Sword, by these Names—<term>Abrahach</term>, <term>Abrach</term>, <term>Abracadabra</term>, <term>Yod He Vau He</term>—that you serve me as a source of strength and defense in all Magical Operations against all my enemies, visible and invisible.

> I conjure you again by the Holy and Indivisible Name of <term>El</term> <note>God</note>, strong and wonderful; by the Name <term>Shaddai</term> <note>Almighty</note>; and by these names: <term>Qadosch</term>, <term>Qadosch</term>, <term>Qadosch</term> <note>Holy, Holy, Holy</note>, <term>Adonai Elohim Tzabaoth</term> <note>Lord God of Hosts</note>, <term>Emanuel</term> <note>God with us</note>, the First and the Last, Wisdom, Way, Life, Truth, Chief, Speech, Word, Splendor, Light, Sun, Fountain, Glory, the Stone of the Wise <note>A reference to the Philosopher's Stone</note>, Virtue, Shepherd, Priest, and Immortal Messiah <note>original: "Messiach"</note>. By these names, then, and by the other names, I conjure you, O Sword, that you serve me as a protection in all adversities. Amen.

When this is finished, you shall also wrap it in silk like all the other instruments, once it has been properly purified and consecrated by the ceremonies required for the perfection of all Magical Arts and Operations.

Three* other swords should be made for the use of the disciples.

The first one should have the name <term>Cardiel</term> or <term>Gabriel</term> on the pommel (*see Figure 71*); on the <term>Lamen</term> <note>A decorative plate or protective amulet</note> of the guard, write <term>Region</term> (*Figure 72*); and on the blade, write <term>Panoraim Heamesin</term> (*Figure 73*).

The second sword should have the name <term>Auriel</term> on the pommel (*Figure 74*)...

During the day and hour of either Mars <note>Tuesday</note> or Venus <note>Friday</note>, you shall engrave the characters shown upon it. After sprinkling it with holy water and censing <note>perfuming with incense</note> it, you shall repeat the following prayer over it:

### PRAYER.

> ASOPHIEL, ASOPHIEL, ASOPHIEL, Five-Lettered Name <note>original: "PENTAGRAMMATON"</note>, Immortal One <note>original: "ATHANATOS," from Greek</note>, I Am That I Am <note>original: "EHEIEH ASHER EHEIEH," from Hebrew</note>, Holy, Holy, Holy <note>original: "QADOSCH, QADOSCH, QADOSCH," from Hebrew</note>; O Eternal God, and my Father, bless this instrument prepared in Your honor, so that it may only serve a good purpose and end, for Your glory. Amen.

Having perfumed it again, you shall put it aside for use. The needle may be consecrated in the same way.

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER IX.

->Concerning the Construction of the Circle.<-

<margin>This chapter is only found in the British Library's Additional Manuscript 10,862.</margin>
Having chosen a place for preparing and building the Circle, and after preparing everything necessary for the successful completion of the operations, take the Sickle or <term>Scimitar</term> <note>a curved ritual blade</note> of Art and stick it into the center of the spot where the Circle is to be made. Then, take a cord nine feet long, fasten one end of it to the Sickle, and use the other end to trace the circumference of the Circle. This may be marked with either the Sword or the Knife with the black hilt. 

Next, mark out four regions within the Circle—namely, towards the East, West, South, and North—and place symbols in each. Beyond the limits of this first Circle, use the consecrated Knife or Sword to draw another Circle, but leave an opening in it toward the North so that you may enter and leave the Circle of Art. Beyond this second circle, draw a third Circle one foot away using the same instrument, again leaving an opening for entrance and exit that aligns with the opening in the previous one. 

Beyond this, make a fourth Circle another foot away. Outside of these two outer circles—which surround the Circle of Art but share the same center—you shall draw **Pentagrams** <note>five-pointed stars</note> containing the Symbols and Names of the Creator, so that they surround the circles already drawn. 

Outside of all these circles, you shall draw a square, and then another square outside of that one. Position them so that the corners of the inner square touch the centers of the sides of the outer square, and the corners of the outer square point toward the four cardinal directions of the universe: East, West, North, and South. At the four corners of each square, touching them, you shall draw smaller circles. Inside these, place standing <term>censers</term> <note>ornate containers for burning incense</note> with lit charcoal and sweet-smelling incense.

<margin>The manuscript uses the word "Magus" <note>a sorcerer or practitioner of magic</note> rather than "Magister" <note>Latin for "Master" or "Teacher"</note>.</margin>
Once these things are done, let the **Magus** of Art gather his disciples, and let him encourage, reassure, and hearten them. He should lead them into the Circle of Art and station them there toward the four cardinal directions, urging them to fear nothing and to stay in their assigned places. Furthermore, let each of the companions carry a sword in addition to the Sword of Art, which they must hold unsheathed in their hands. Then, let the Magus leave the Circle to light the censers and place consecrated incense upon them, as described in the chapter on fumigations <note>the ritual use of smoke and incense</note>. He shall then hold the censers in his—

<summary>This page provides detailed geometric instructions for drawing a complex ritual circle, including concentric rings, pentagrams, and squares, and describes the initial stations of the Magus and his disciples.</summary>
<keywords>ritual circle, Magus, geometry, pentagrams, censers, incense, sacred space, protection symbols</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the construction and ritual use of the magical circle, following the geometric layout described on page 103.</meta>

<page-num>104</page-num>
<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

...hold and light it, then place it in the designated area. He should now enter the Circle and carefully close the openings left for entry. He should warn his assistants <note>original: "Disciples"</note> once more, and then take the Ritual Trumpet <note>original: "Trumpet of Art"</note>—prepared as described in the chapter concerning that tool—and use incense to bless the Circle toward the four cardinal directions of the universe.

After this, the Magician <note>original: "Magus"</note> should begin his incantations, having placed the sickle, sword, or another ritual tool upright in the ground at his feet. After sounding the trumpet as previously taught, he should summon the spirits; if necessary, he may command <note>original: "conjure"</note> them as described in the First Book. Once he has achieved his desired goal, he should give them permission to leave.

Below is the diagram of the Circle (see Figure 81). Anyone who enters it will be as safe as if they were inside a fortified castle, and nothing will be able to harm them.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER X.

### CONCERNING INCENSE, RITUAL SMOKE <note>original: "Suffumigations"</note>, PERFUMES, ODORS, AND SIMILAR THINGS WHICH ARE USED IN MAGICAL ARTS.

There are many kinds of incense, ritual smoke, and perfumes which are made for and offered to the spirits; those with a sweet odor are for the good spirits, while those with an unpleasant smell <note>original: "evil savour"</note> are for the evil spirits. 

For perfumes with a pleasant odor, take aloes, nutmeg, benzoin <note>original: "gum benjamin"</note>, and musk, and make a mixture which will produce a pleasant fragrance.

**EDITOR'S NOTE.**—The advanced student of the occult and *Disciple of Magical Art* uses "Vale of Kashmar," a famous Oriental perfume, today instead of the spices mentioned here. Vale of Kashmar perfume, because of its pure and beautiful fragrance, strength, and power <note>original: "virtue"</note>, is believed to possess wonderful power to attract *Good Spirits* and banish *Evil Ones*. For this very reason, it is now generally used in all invocations instead of the spices mentioned above. The disciple, of course, can choose which they prefer, but if it is not easy or convenient to obtain the spices, you may send to Messrs. De Laurence, Scott & Co. for *Order No. 504*—a bottle of Vale of Kashmar, *The Great Oriental Perfume*. This substance gives off a subtle, powerful, and beautifully fragrant Oriental odor.

For a suitable ritual smoke, you may burn *Temple Incense*, as it gives off a most fragrant odor which seems to possess the power to attract *Good Spirits* and force *Evil Ones* to leave you. Over this incense, you shall say:

->THE EXORCISM OF TEMPLE INCENSE.<-

> O God of Abraham, God of Isaac, and God of Jacob, deign to bless this fragrant incense so that it may receive strength, quality, and power to attract Good Spirits, and to banish and drive away <note>original: "cause to retire"</note> all hostile phantoms. Through You, O Most Holy Adonai <note>Hebrew: "Lord"</note>, Who lives and reigns through all the ages <note>original: "unto the Ages of the Ages"</note>. Amen.

> I exorcise you, O spirit impure and unclean, you who are a hostile phantom, in the Name of God, that you leave this Temple Incense, you...

<summary>This page explains the use of incense and perfumes to attract good spirits and repel evil ones during a ritual. It includes a specific commercial recommendation for "Vale of Kashmar" perfume and the beginning of an exorcism prayer over the incense.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the consecration of ritual incense and perfumes, providing the final prayers for the spices and the specific exorcism used to prepare the ritual fire.</meta>

<page-num>106</page-num>
<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

...and all your deceits, so that it may be consecrated and sanctified in the name of God Almighty. May the Holy Spirit of God grant protection and power <note>original: "virtue"</note> to those who use *Temple Incense*; and may the hostile and *Evil Spirit* and *Phantom* never be able to enter into it, through the Ineffable Name of God Almighty. So be it <note>original: "Amen"</note>.

> O Lord, graciously condescend to bless and to sanctify this *Sacred Incense* so that it may be a remedy for mankind, for the health of body and of soul, through the invocation of Your Holy Name. May all creatures who receive the odor of this *Incense* and of these spices receive health of body and of soul, through Him who has formed the ages. So be it <note>original: "Amen"</note>.

After this, you shall sprinkle the various spices (or the perfumed handkerchief mentioned in the footnote below) with the "Water of the Art" <note>consecrated ritual water</note>, and you shall place them aside in a piece of silk as in other cases, or in a box intended for that purpose, so that you may have them ready and prepared for use when necessary.

When you wish to use the *Temple Incense*, you shall kindle a fire of fresh incense in an incense burner <note>original: "Incense Burner"</note>. Once the incense is lit, you shall say the following over it before putting the spices or the perfumed handkerchief beside the burner:

### THE EXORCISM OF THE FIRE.

> I command you <note>original: "exorcise thee"</note>, O Creature of Fire, by Him through whom all things have been made, so that every kind of phantom may retreat from you and be unable to harm or deceive in any way, through the invocation of the Most High Creator of all. So be it <note>original: "Amen"</note>.

> Bless, O Lord All-Powerful and All-Merciful, this Creature of Fire, so that being blessed by You, it may be for the honor and glory of Your Most Holy Name, and that it may work no hindrance or evil to those who use it. Through You, O Eternal and Almighty Lord, and through Your Most Holy Name. So be it <note>original: "Amen"</note>.

This being done, you shall put the spices upon the fire and perform whatever ritual perfumes and incense-burnings <note>original: "suffumigations"</note> you require.

Over ritual burnings of an unpleasant odor <note>original: "Fumigations of evil odour"</note>, you shall say:

> <term>My Lord</term> <note>original: "Adonai"</note>, <term>Lazai</term>, <term>Dalmai</term>, <term>Aima</term>, <term>God</term> <note>original: "Elohi"</note>, O Holy Father, grant us help, favor, and grace by the invocation of Your Holy Name, so that these things may serve as an aid in all that we wish to perform with them; let all deceit leave them, and let them be blessed and sanctified through Your Name. So be it <note>original: "Amen"</note>.

---

\* "Vale of Kashmar," the famous Oriental perfume, may be used by sprinkling it on a clean handkerchief and placing it beside the incense instead of the spices, if you prefer it.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XI.

### CONCERNING THE WATER AND THE HYSSOP.

If it is necessary to sprinkle anything required for the Magic Arts <note>original: "the Art"</note> with water, it should be done using a ritual sprinkler.

Prepare a censer on the day and during the hour of Mercury <note>Wednesday, during specific planetary hours believed to empower communication and ritual tools</note> with the fragrant spices of the Craft. After this, you shall take a vessel made of brass, of lead (lacquered inside and out), or of earthenware. Fill it with very clear spring water and take some salt, saying these words over the salt:

> Lord of Hosts <note>original: "TZABAOTH"</note>, Anointed One <note>original: "MESSIACH"</note>, God With Us <note>original: "EMANUEL"</note>, Mighty God <note>original: "ELOHIM GIBOR"</note>, the Four-Letter Name <note>original: "YOD HE VAU HE," the Tetragrammaton</note>; O God, Who are the Truth and the Life, deign to bless and sanctify this salt <note>original: "Creature of Salt"</note> so that it may serve us as help, protection, and assistance in this Craft <note>original: "Art"</note>, experiment, and operation, and may it be a support to us.

After this, throw the salt into the vessel containing the water and recite the following Psalms: 102, 54, 6, and 67.

<margin>?</margin>

You shall then make a ritual sprinkler <note>historically called an aspergillum</note> out of vervain, fennel, lavender, sage, valerian, mint, garden basil, rosemary, and hyssop. These should be gathered on the day and during the hour of Mercury while the moon is waxing <note>original: "in her increase"</note>. Bind these herbs together with a thread spun by a young maiden. Engrave the symbols <note>original: "characters"</note> shown in *Figure 82* on one side of the handle, and those given in *Figure 83* on the other side.

After this, you may use the water, employing the sprinkler whenever it is necessary. Know that wherever you sprinkle this water, it will drive away all phantoms <note>hostile spirits or illusions</note>, and they will be unable to hinder or annoy anyone. Use this same water for all the preparations required by the Magic Arts.

<summary>This chapter details the ritual preparation of "holy water" and a herbal sprinkler used to purify ritual spaces and tools, specifically to drive away malevolent spirits.</summary>

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XII.

### CONCERNING THE LIGHT AND THE FIRE.

It has always been the custom among all nations to use fire and light in sacred matters. For this reason, the Master of the Craft <note>original: "Master of the Art"</note> should also employ them in sacred rites. In addition to using them for reading the Incantations <note>original: "Conjurations"</note> and for burning the incense, lights are necessary within the Circle during all operations.

For this reason, he should make candles of virgin wax <note>pure, unused beeswax</note> during the day and hour of Mercury <note>Wednesday, during specific planetary hours</note>. The wicks should have been made by a young girl, and the candles should be created when the moon is waxing <note>original: "in her increase"</note>. Each should weigh half a pound. On them, you shall engrave these characters with the Ritual Dagger or the Engraving Tool <note>original: "Burin of Art"</note>. (*See Figure 84.*)

*After this, you shall repeat over the candles Psalms 151, 103, and 107, and say:*

> O Lord God, Who governs all things by Your Almighty Power, give to me, a poor sinner, understanding and knowledge to do only that which is pleasing to You. Grant me the grace to fear, adore, love, praise, and give thanks to You with true and sincere faith and perfect charity. Grant, O Lord, before I die and descend into the realms beneath, and before the fiery flame shall devour me, that Your Grace may not leave me, O Lord of my Soul. Amen.

*After this, you shall add:*

> I exorcise you, O Being <note>original: "Creature"</note> of wax, by Him Who alone has created all things by His Word, and by the virtue of Him Who is pure truth, that you cast out from yourself every phantom, perversion, and deceit of the Enemy. May the Virtue and Power of God enter into you, so that you may give us light and chase far from us all fear or terror.

After this, you shall sprinkle them with the Holy Water of the Craft and cense them with the usual perfumes.

*And when you wish to light them, you shall say:*

> I exorcise you, O Being <note>original: "Creature"</note> of Fire, in the Name of the Sovereign and Eternal Lord, by His Unspeakable <note>original: "Ineffable"</note> Name, which is YOD, HE, VAU, HE <note>The four Hebrew letters of the Tetragrammaton, the name of God</note>; by the Name IAH <note>A shortened form of the divine name</note>; and by the Name of Power EL <note>Hebrew for "God"</note>; that you may enlighten the hearts of all the Spirits which we shall call into this Circle, so that they may appear before us without fraud or deceit, through Him Who has created all things.

Then you shall take a square lantern with panes of crystal glass and place the lighted candle inside to read by, to form the Circle, or for any other purpose for which you require it.

---

<note>Special waxen candles may be obtained from the publishers, de Laurence, Scott & Co., if it is not convenient to construct the kind mentioned above. Many students are using these candles with good results. See Order No. 131.</note>

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XIII.

### CONCERNING THE PRECEPTS OF THE ART.

He who has attained the rank or degree of Exorcist—which we are usually accustomed to call Magus or Master <note>original: "Magus or Master." A footnote in the original text states: "This Chapter is only given in 10,862 Add. MSS," referring to a specific manuscript in the British Library.</note> according to his grade—whenever he desires to undertake any operation, should put away all impurity from himself for the nine days immediately preceding the start of the work. He must prepare himself in secret during these days and prepare all necessary things; during this time, all these items should be made, consecrated, and exorcised.

Once these tasks are duly completed, let him go on the day and at the hour the work begins to the place set aside for it—as has been previously mentioned in the section concerning the formation of the Circle. He must instruct his disciples not to move from their assigned places for any reason whatsoever. Then the Magus should encourage them with a bold and confident voice as follows:

->THE EXHORTATION OF THE COMPANIONS.<-

Do not fear, my beloved companions, for we are drawing near to our desired goal. Therefore, since everything has been done correctly and the invocations <note>original: "Conjurations"</note> and exorcisms have been performed diligently, you shall behold the King of Kings, the Emperor of Emperors, and other kings, princes, and high spirits <note>original: "Majesties"</note> with them. They will be accompanied by a great crowd of followers and all sorts of musical instruments. However, neither the Magus nor his disciples should feel any fear. 

Then the Magus should say:
> I urge you by these Holy Names of God—**Elohim** <note>God</note>, **Adonai** <note>Lord</note>, and **Agla** <note>a Hebrew acronym for "You are mighty forever, O Lord"</note>—that none of you now dare to move or step outside your assigned positions.

After saying this, the Magus and his disciples should uncover the Holy Pentacles <term>Pentacles: Talismans engraved with sacred names and symbols used to constrain spirits.</term> and show them toward each of the four cardinal directions. When they are shown in each direction, there will be loud noises and rushing sounds. 

Then the Emperor of the spirits shall say to you: "From the time of the Great Addus <note>Addus: An obscure figure in Solomonic lore, possibly referring to an ancient king or a legendary practitioner of the art.</note> until now, there has not been an Exorcist who could look upon my person. If those things† <note>the Pentacles</note> which you have shown us had not been made, you would not be seeing me now. But seeing that—"

***

"You have powerfully called us, as I believe, by the rites derived from Solomon—rites which few of your comrades, or <term>Exorcists</term> <note>original: "Exorcisers"</note>, possess. They compel us against our will, and I therefore say to you that we wish to be obedient in all matters."

Then the <term>Magus</term> <note>A master magician or the lead practitioner of the ritual.</note> shall place the petitions for himself and his companions—which should be written down clearly on <term>virgin card</term> <note>original: "virgin card," referring to unused parchment or paper specifically prepared for magical use to ensure spiritual purity.</note> or paper—beyond the <term>Circle</term> toward the King or Prince of the <term>Spirits</term>. The spirit King will receive it and take counsel with his leaders. After this, he will return the card, saying: "That which you desire is accomplished; let your will be performed, and all your demands fulfilled."

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER XIV.

#### OF THE PEN, INK, AND COLORS.

All things employed for writing, and so forth, in this Art should be prepared in the following manner:
You shall take a male gosling, from which you shall pluck the third feather of the right wing, and while plucking it you shall say:
> ADRAI, HAHLII, TAMAII, TILONAS, ATHAMAS, ZIANOR, **My Lord** <note>original: "ADONAI"</note>, banish from this pen all deceit and error, so that it may have the power and effectiveness to write all that I desire. Amen.

After this, you shall sharpen it with the <term>penknife of the Art</term> <note>A knife specially consecrated for ritual use in preparing magical tools.</note>, perfume it with incense, sprinkle it with holy water, and place it aside in a silken cloth.
You shall have an inkstand made of clay or any suitable material, and in the day and hour of Mercury, you shall engrave on it with the <term>Burin of Art</term> <note>A specialized engraving tool used by the practitioner.</note> these names: Yod, He, Vau, He <note>The four Hebrew letters of the Tetragrammaton, the sacred name of God.</note>, Metatron, **The Lord, The Lord, The Lord** <note>original: "Iah Iah Iah"</note>, **Holy** <note>original: "Qadosch"</note>, **God of Hosts** <note>original: "Elohim Tzabaoth"</note> (*see Figure 85*); and while putting the ink into it, you shall say:
> I exorcise you, O Creature of Ink, by **Destroyer** <note>original: "ANAIRETON," likely from the Greek for 'one who destroys.'</note>, by **Imitator** <note>original: "SIMULATOR," likely from the Latin for 'one who feigns.'</note>, and by the name **My Lord** <note>original: "ADONAI"</note>, and by the name of Him through whom all things were made, that you may be an aid and help to me in all things which I wish to perform with your assistance.

As it sometimes happens that it is necessary to write with some noble color, it is well to have a new and clean box in which to keep them. The principal colors will be Yellow or Gold, Red, Sky or Azure Blue, Green, and Brown; and any other colors that may be required. You shall exorcise, perfume, and sprinkle them in the usual manner.

# BOOK TWO

## CHAPTER XV

### OF THE PEN OF THE SWALLOW AND OF THE CROW

Take the feather of a swallow or of a crow, and before plucking it, you shall say:
> May Holy MICHAEL, the Archangel of God, and MIDAEL and MIRAEL, the Chiefs and Captains of the Celestial Army, be my aid in the operation I am about to perform, so that I may write with this all things which are necessary, and that all the experiments which I begin with it may—through you and through your names—be perfected by the power of the Most High Creator. Amen.

After this, you shall sharpen <note>original: "point," referring to the process of cutting a quill into a nib.</note> and finish the pen with the <term>Knife of the Art</term> <note>A knife specially consecrated for the preparation of magical instruments.</note>. Using the pen and ink of the art, you shall write upon its side the Name **The Destroyer** <note>original: "ANAIRETON," likely from the Greek word for "the one who destroys" or "takes away."</note> (*see Figure 86*), and you shall recite over it the following Psalms: 133 and 117.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XVI.

### OF THE BLOOD OF THE BAT, PIGEON, AND OTHER ANIMALS.

Take a living bat and exorcise it in this way:—

->THE EXORCISM OF THE BAT.<-

CAMIACH, EOMIAHE, EMIAL, MACBAL, EMOII, ZAZEAN, MAIPHIAT, ZACRATH, TENDAC, VULAMAHI; by these Most Holy Names, and the other names of angels which are written in the *Book of the Heavens* <note>original: "ASSAMAIAN," referring to the *Sepher Ha-Shamaiim*</note>, I conjure you, O Bat (or whatever animal it may be), to assist me in this operation. I command you by the True God, the Holy God, the God who has created you, and by Adam, who gave your true name to you and to all other living creatures.

After this, take the needle or another suitable <term>Instrument of Art</term> <note>A tool specifically consecrated and prepared for ritual use.</note>, as will be explained later on, and pierce the bat in the vein located in the right wing. Collect the blood in a small vessel, over which you shall say:—

> Almighty My Lord <note>original: "ADONAI"</note>, [Arathron, Ashai], God <note>original: "ELOHIM"</note>, My God <note>original: "ELOHI"</note>, Most High <note>original: "ELION"</note>, I Am That I Am <note>original: "ASHER EHEIEH"</note>, Almighty <note>original: "SHADDAI"</note>, O God the Lord, immaculate, unchangeable, God With Us <note>original: "EMANUEL"</note>, Anointed One <note>original: "MESSIACH"</note>, **YOD, HE, VAU, HE** <note>The four-letter name of God, the Tetragrammaton.</note>, be my aid, so that this blood may have power and effectiveness in everything I desire and in everything I demand.

Perfume it <note>This likely refers to censing the blood with ritual incense.</note> and keep it for use.

The blood of other winged animals may be taken in the same manner, following the proper solemnities.

---

I cannot too strongly emphasize to the readers of this volume that the use of blood is more or less connected with Black Magic, and that it should be avoided as much as possible.—DR. de LAURENCE. <note>L. W. de Laurence was a prolific 20th-century publisher of occult texts who often added his own warnings and commentary to older grimoires.</note>

\* The *Sepher Ha-Shamaiim*, or "Book of the Heavens."

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XVII.

### OF VIRGIN PARCHMENT, OR VIRGIN PAPER, AND HOW IT SHOULD BE PREPARED.

Virgin *Parchment Paper* <note>Although the text uses the word "paper," it refers specifically to parchment—the processed skin of an animal—used for ritual writing.</note> is made from the skin of stillborn lambs; it must be new, pure, clean, and exorcised, never having been used for any other purpose.

Genuine *Virgin Parchment* is necessary in many <term>Magical Operations</term> <note>Specific occult rituals or ceremonies designed to produce a particular effect.</note> and should be properly prepared and consecrated. There are two kinds: one called *Virgin*, the other *Unborn*. *Virgin Parchment* is taken from an animal that has not yet reached the age of breeding, whether it is a ram, a kid, or another animal.

*Unborn Parchment* is taken from an animal that has been removed from its mother’s womb before its time.

Take whichever of these two classes of animals you please, provided only that it is a male and is taken on the day and hour of <term>Mercury</term> <note>In planetary magic, Mercury is associated with Wednesday and rules over writing, communication, and intellect.</note>. Take it to a secret place where no one may see you at work. You shall have a marsh reed cut with a single stroke of a new knife, and you shall strip the leaves from it while repeating this Conjuration:

->THE CONJURATION OF THE REED.<-

> I conjure you by the Creator of all things and by the King of Angels, whose Name is God Almighty <note>original: "EL SHADDAI"</note>, that you receive strength and virtue to skin this animal and to construct the parchment whereon I may write the Holy Names of God; and may it acquire such great power that everything I shall write or do may achieve its effect, through Him who lives for the eternal ages. Amen.

Before cutting the reed, recite Psalm 72.

After this, with the <term>Knife of the Art</term> <note>A specialized ritual knife consecrated for magical use.</note>, you shall fashion the reed into the shape of a knife, and upon it you shall write these Names: **AGLA**, **Lord** <note>original: "ADONAI"</note>, **My God** <note>original: "ELOHI"</note> (see Figure 87), through whom the work of this knife may be accomplished. Then you shall say:

> O God, who drew Moses, your well-beloved and your chosen one, from among the reeds on the marshy banks of the Nile and from the waters while he was yet a child, grant to me through your great mercy and compassion that this reed may receive power and virtue to achieve what I desire through your Holy Name and the names of your Holy Angels. Amen.

This being done, you shall begin to skin the animal with this knife, whether it is <term>Virgin</term> or <term>Unborn</term>, saying:

->RADIANCE, SPLENDOR, TALMAÏ, LORD, ALMIGHTY, THE FOUR-LETTER NAME <note>original: "ZOHAR, ZIO, TALMAÏ, ADONAI, SHADDAI, TETRAGRAMMATON"</note>, and<-

<summary>This page describes the ritual requirements for "virgin parchment" and the construction of a ceremonial reed knife needed to flay the animal skin for magical use.</summary>

<keywords>Virgin Parchment, Unborn Parchment, Reed Knife, El Shaddai, Mercury, Ritual Flaying, Moses, Psalm 72, Magic Tools</keywords>

--- Page 141 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for preparing "virgin parchment" begun on page 114, detailing the ritual cleansing, stretching, and consecration of the animal skin.</meta>

<page-num>115</page-num>

<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

You Holy Angels of God; be present, and grant power and virtue unto this parchment, and may it be consecrated by you, so that all things which I shall write thereon shall obtain their effect. Amen.

The animal being skinned <note>original: "flayed"</note>, take Salt, and say the following over it:

God of Gods, and Lord of Lords, Who has created all things from Nothingness <note>original: "Negative Existence"; in the Kabbalah, this refers to the state of God before manifestation.</note>, deign to bless and sanctify this Salt, so that in placing it upon this parchment which I wish to make, it may have such virtue that whatsoever I may write on it hereafter may attain its desired end. Amen.

Afterwards, rub the said parchment with the exorcised salt, and leave it in the Sun to absorb this salt for the space of an entire day. Then take a large earthen vessel glazed inside and out, and around the outside of it you shall write the characters shown in *Figure 88*.

After this, you shall put powdered lime into the vessel, saying:

OROII, ZARON, ZAINON, ZEVARON, ZAHIPHIL, ELION <note>These are magical names or "words of power" often used in medieval evocations.</note>, be present and bless this work so that it may attain the desired effect, through the King of the Heavens, and the God of the Angels. Amen.

Take then exorcised Water and pour it upon the lime, and place the skin inside for three days, after which you shall take it out and scrape the lime and adhering flesh from it with the <term>Knife of Reed</term> <note>A specialized ritual tool described on the previous page, made from a swamp reed.</note>.

After this, you shall cut, with a single stroke, a Wand of Hazel long enough for you to form a Circle with it. Take also a cord spun by a young maiden, and small stones or pebbles from a brook, pronouncing these words:

O Lord <note>original: "God Adonai"</note>, Holy and Powerful Father, put virtue into these stones, so that they may serve to stretch this parchment and to drive away all deception from it, and may it obtain virtue by Your Almighty Power.

After this, having stretched the parchment upon the Circle and bound it with the cord and stones, you shall say:

YOU ARE MIGHTY FOREVER, O LORD <note>original: "AGLA," a Hebrew acronym for "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai."</note>, [the Divine Name] <note>original: "YOD, HE, VAU, HE," the four letters of the Tetragrammaton.</note>, GOD <note>original: "IAH"</note>, GOD WITH US <note>original: "EMANUEL"</note>, bless and preserve this parchment, so that no haunting spirit <note>original: "Phantasm"</note> may enter it.

Let it dry in this way for three days in a dark and shady place, then cut the cord with the <term>Knife of Art</term> <note>A consecrated ritual knife used in magical ceremonies, often featuring a black handle.</note>, and detach the Parchment from the Circle, saying:

ANTOR, ANCOR, TURLOS, BEODONOS, PHAIAR, APHARCAR, be present as a guard for this Parchment.

Then perfume it with incense and keep it wrapped in silk, ready for use.

No woman, if her monthly period <note>original: "flowers"</note> is upon her, should be permitted to see this Parchment; otherwise, it will lose its power. He who makes it should be pure, clean, and prepared.

But if the preparation of the aforementioned parchment seems too tedious, you may make it in the following manner, though it is not as good:

Take any Parchment and exorcise it; prepare a censer with perfumes; write upon the parchment the characters in *Figure 89*, hold it over the Incense, and say:
