# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XVIII.

### OF WAX AND VIRGIN EARTH.

Wax and Virgin Earth <note>In magical practice, "virgin" materials are those that are pure, natural, and have never been used for any secular purpose.</note> are also used in many magical operations, whether to make images, candles, or other things; therefore, they should never have been put to any other use. The earth should be dug up with your own hands and made into a paste without touching it with any tool <note>original: "instrument"</note> whatsoever, so that it is not defiled by it.

The wax should be taken from bees which have only made it for the first time <note>This refers to wax from a new swarm or a fresh hive that has not been processed for common use.</note>, and it should never have been used for any other purpose. When you wish to use one or the other, you shall repeat the following conjuration before starting the work:

->CONJURATION.<-

> EXTABOR, HETABOR, SITTACIBOR, ADONAI, ONZO, ZOMEN, MENOR, ASMODAL, ASCOBAI, COMATOS, ERIONAS, PROFAS, ALKOMAS, CONAMAS, PAPUENDOS, OSIANDOS, ESPIACENT, DAMNATH, EHERES, GOLADES, TELANTES, COPHI, ZADES <note>These are "Words of Power" or names of spirits common in medieval grimoires; they are intended to be spoken for their phonetic ritual effect rather than translated.</note>, you Angels of God be present, for I call upon <note>original: "invoke"</note> you in my work, so that through you it may find power <note>original: "virtue"</note> and completion. Amen.

After this, repeat Psalms 131, 15, 102, 8, 84, 68, 72, 133, 113, 126, 46, 47, 22, 51, 130, 139, 49, 110, and 53 <note>The original text lists these using Roman numerals.</note>; and say:

> I exorcise you, O Creature of Wax (or of Earth), so that through the Holy Name of God and His Holy Angels you may receive a blessing, and that you may be sanctified and blessed, and obtain the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> which we desire, through the Most Holy Name of **ADONAI** <note>Hebrew: "The Lord."</note>. Amen.

Sprinkle the wax <note>Likely referring to the ritual sprinkling of holy water or an infusion of herbs.</note> and set it aside for use; but take note that the earth, which should be dug up with your hands, should be prepared every time you have a need for it.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XIX.

### CONCERNING THE NEEDLE AND OTHER IRON INSTRUMENTS.

There are several steel instruments necessary in various operations, such as a needle to prick or to sew, and a burin <note>A burin is a specialized steel tool used for engraving or cutting into metal.</note> or instrument with which to engrave, and so on.

You shall make such instruments on the day and hour of Jupiter <note>In planetary magic, Jupiter is associated with Thursday; the "hour" refers to specific times of the day calculated by dividing the time between sunrise and sunset into twelve "planetary hours."</note>, and when it is finished you shall say:

> I conjure you, O instrument of steel, by God the Father Almighty; by the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> of the heavens, of the stars, and of the angels who preside over them; by the power of stones, herbs, and animals; by the power of hail, snow, and wind; so that you receive such power that you may achieve, without failure <note>original: "deceit"</note>, the end which I desire in all things where I shall use you; through God the Creator of the Ages and Emperor of the Angels. Amen.

Afterward repeat Psalms 3, 9, 31, 42, 60, 51, and 130 <note>The original text uses Roman numerals: iii, ix, xxxi, xlii, lx, li, and cxxx.</note>.

Cense <note>original: "perfume," referring to the ritual use of incense.</note> it with the perfumes of the Art, sprinkle it with exorcised water, wrap it in silk, and say:

> DANI, ZUMECH, AGALMATUROD, GADIEL, PANI, CANELOAS, MEROD, GAMIDOI, BALDOI, METRATOR <note>These are names of power or angels intended to be spoken for their ritual sound; they are preserved from the original grimoire tradition.</note>, most holy angels, be present as a guard for this instrument.

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER XX.

#### CONCERNING THE SILKEN CLOTH.

When any tool of the craft <note>original: "Instrument of the Art"</note> is properly consecrated, it should be wrapped in silk and stored, as previously mentioned.

Take silk of any color except black or grey, and write upon it the words and characters shown in *Figure 90*.

Cense it <note>original: "Perfume," referring to the ritual use of incense.</note> with sweet-smelling incense, sprinkle it with holy water, and recite Psalms 82, 72, 134, and 64 <note>The original text uses Roman numerals: lxxxii, lxxii, cxxxiv, and lxiv.</note>.

Afterward, you shall set it aside for seven days with sweet spices; then you shall use this silk to wrap all the tools of the craft <note>original: "instruments of the Art"</note>.

# BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XXI.

### REGARDING CHARACTERS AND THE CONSECRATION OF THE MAGICAL BOOK.

Whenever you must write characters <note>Magical symbols or sigils used in rituals.</note> in any operation and you fear you might fail, do this: At the beginning, write the name I AM THAT I AM <note>original Hebrew: "EHEIEH ASHER EHEIEH"</note> (*Figure 91*), and at the end write the name The Infinite <note>original Hebrew: "AIN SOPH"</note> (*Figure 92*). Write what you wish between these names. If you have a specific task to perform, carry these written names upon a silk wrapper, and you shall say over them:

> Most Wise and Most High Creator of all things, I pray to You for Your grace and mercy, that You may grant such virtue and power to these Holy Names, that You may keep these characters from all deceit and error, through You, O Most Holy Lord <note>original Hebrew: "Adonai"</note>. Amen.

After repeating this, you shall write the necessary characters; you will not fail but will achieve your desired goal.

### THE CONSECRATION OF THE BOOK.

Make a book containing sixteen pages from *virgin parchment* <term>Parchment made from the skin of an animal that has not reached maturity or reproduced, specially prepared to be ritually pure.</term> and write in it, using red ink, the prayers for all the operations, the names of the angels in the form of litanies <term>A formal prayer consisting of a series of petitions or invocations.</term>, and their seals and characters. When this is done, you shall consecrate the book to God and to the pure spirits in the following manner:

You shall set a small table covered with a white cloth in the designated place. Lay the book open there at the *Great Pentacle* <note>A complex magical symbol representing the authority of the magician.</note>, which should be drawn on the first leaf of the book. Having lit a lamp suspended above the center of the table, you shall surround the table with a white curtain. Clothe yourself in the proper ritual garments, and holding the book open, repeat the following prayer on your knees with great humility:

(For the prayer beginning "Lord God <note>original Hebrew: "Adonai Elohim"</note>," etc., see Book I, Chapter XIV, where it is given in full.)

Afterward, you shall cense the book <note>original: "incense it," meaning to pass it through the smoke of ritual incense.</note> with the incense appropriate to the planet and the day. Replace the book on the table, taking care that the fire of the lamp is kept burning continuously during the operation and keeping the curtains closed. Repeat the same ceremony for seven days, beginning on Saturday. Each day, perfume the book with the incense proper to the planet ruling that day and hour, and ensure the lamp burns both day and night. After the—

After this, you shall shut the Book in a small drawer under the table, made specifically for it, until you have occasion to use it. Every time you wish to use it, clothe yourself in your ritual garments, light the lamp, and repeat the aforementioned prayer, “*Adonai Elohim*” <note>original Hebrew: "Adonai Elohim," meaning "Lord God."</note> etc., while on your knees.

In the Consecration of the Book, it is also necessary to summon all the Angels whose Names are written in it in the form of litanies <term>A formal prayer consisting of a series of petitions or invocations.</term>. You must do this with devotion. Even if the Angels and Spirits do not appear during the Consecration of the Book, do not be surprised. They are of a pure nature and consequently have much difficulty familiarizing themselves with humans, who are inconsistent and impure. However, if the ceremonies and characters <term>Magical symbols or sigils used in rituals.</term> are carried out correctly, devoutly, and with perseverance, the spirits will be compelled to come. Eventually, it will happen that at your first invocation, you will be able to see and communicate with them. But I advise you to undertake nothing unclean or impure, for then your persistence—far from attracting them—will only serve to drive them away from you; after that, it will be exceedingly difficult for you to attract them for use for pure purposes.

## BOOK TWO.

### CHAPTER XXII.

### CONCERNING SACRIFICES TO THE SPIRITS, AND HOW THEY SHOULD BE MADE.

In many operations, it is necessary to make some sort of sacrifice to the spirits <note>original: "Demons." In this historical context, the term often refers to any supernatural entity or "daimon," whether good or evil.</note> in various ways. Sometimes white animals are sacrificed to the good spirits and black animals to the evil ones. Such sacrifices consist of the blood and sometimes of the flesh.

Those who sacrifice animals, of whatever kind they may be, should select those which are "virgin" <term>In ritual magic, this refers to young animals that have not yet mated, symbolizing purity and potential.</term>, as these are more agreeable to the spirits and make them more obedient.

When blood is to be sacrificed, it should also be drawn from virgin four-legged animals or birds. But before offering the sacrifice <note>original: "oblation"</note>, say:

> May this sacrifice, which we find it appropriate to offer to you, noble and lofty beings, be agreeable and pleasing to your desires; be ready to obey us, and you shall receive even greater ones.

Then perfume and sprinkle it according to the rules of the Art <term>The "Art" refers to the specialized practice of ceremonial magic.</term>.

When it is necessary to make sacrifices of fire—using all the proper ceremonies—they should be made with wood that has qualities specifically related to the spirits being summoned. For example:
* **Juniper or pine** for the spirits of Saturn;
* **Box or oak** for those of Jupiter;
* **Cornel or cedar** for those of Mars;
* **Laurel** for those of the Sun;
* **Myrtle** for those of Venus;
* **Hazel** for those of Mercury;
* **Willow** for those of the Moon.

But when we make sacrifices of food and drink, everything necessary should be prepared outside the circle. The meats should be covered with a fine, clean cloth, and a clean white cloth should also be spread beneath them. Use new bread and good, sparkling wine, ensuring all items correspond to the nature of the Planet being worked with. Animals, such as chickens or pigeons, should be roasted. Above all, you should have a vessel of clear and pure spring water. Before you enter the circle, you shall summon the spirits by their proper names—or at least the leaders among them—saying:

> In whatever place you may be, you spirits who are invited to this feast, come and be ready to receive our offerings, presents, and sacrifices, and you shall have even more agreeable offerings in the future.

First, perfume the room by burning temple incense within it, and sprinkle the food <note>original: "viands"</note> with exorcised water; then begin to conjure the spirits until they arrive.

This is the manner of making sacrifices in all magical arts and operations.

<summary>This chapter details the specific requirements for ritual sacrifices to spirits, including the use of "virgin" animals, planetary wood correspondences for fire, and the proper preparation of food and drink offerings.</summary>

in which it is necessary; and by acting in this way, the spirits will be ready to serve you.

Here ends our "Key." If you thoroughly commit this to your memory, you shall be able—if you wish—to even fly with the wings of the wind <note>original: "fly with the wings of the wind." This is a metaphorical expression for attaining great spiritual power or swiftness through magical means.</note>. But if you pay little attention to this and despise this book, you shall never reach your desired goal in any magical experiment or operation whatsoever.

For in this book is contained all the science of the Magical Art, and it should be strictly kept by you. And this is the end of our "Key," in the name of God the righteous, the merciful, and the eternal, who lives and reigns throughout the ages. Amen.

<page-num>124</page-num>
<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

# ANCIENT FRAGMENT OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON,

## TRANSLATED FROM THE HEBREW BY ELIPHAZ LEVI; *And Given In His "Occult Philosophy"* <note>original: "Philosophe Occulte"</note>—*Series II, Page 136.*

---

I will now give to you the Key of the Kingdom of the Spirits. This Key is the same as that of the Mysterious Numbers of Yetzirah <note>The *Sepher Yetzirah*, or "Book of Formation," is one of the most ancient and fundamental books of the Kabbalah, focusing on the creation of the universe through numbers and letters.</note>. The Spirits are governed by the natural and universal hierarchy of things. Three command three through the medium of three.

There are the Spirits of Above, those of Below, and those of the Center; then if you invert <note>original: "investest." Based on the context of ascending and descending, and Levi's original French "renverses," this means to turn the hierarchy upside down.</note> the Sacred Ladder—if you descend instead of ascending—you will discover the counter-hierarchy of the "Shells" <note>original: "Shells." In Kabbalah, the *Qlippoth* or "Shells" represent unbalanced or evil forces that act as the shadow of the divine emanations.</note>, or of the dead spirits.

Know only that the Principalities of Heaven, the Virtues, and the Powers are not persons, but ranks of honor. They are the degrees of the Sacred Ladder upon which the spirits ascend and descend. Michael, Gabriel, Raphael, and the others are not names but titles.

The first of the numbers is Unity. The first of the divine conceptions called the *Sephiroth* <note>original Hebrew: *Sephiroth*, meaning "emanations" or "spheres" of light.</note> is Kether <note>original Hebrew: *Kether*, meaning "the Crown."</note> or the Crown. The first category of the spirits is that of the Chaioth Ha-Qadesh <note>original Hebrew: *Chaioth Ha-Qadesh*, meaning "Holy Living Creatures."</note>, or the Intelligences of the Divine Tetragram <note>The Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God: YHWH.</note>, whose letters are symbolized by the mysterious animals in the prophecy of Ezekiel. Their empire is that of unity and synthesis. They correspond to the Intelligence. They have for adversaries the *Thamiel* <note>original Hebrew: *Thamiel*, meaning "the double-headed ones."</note> or Double-Headed Ones—the demons of revolt and anarchy—whose two chiefs, ever at war with each other, are *Satan* and *Moloch*.

The second number is two; the second Sephira is Chokmah <note>original Hebrew: *Chokmah*, meaning "Wisdom."</note> or Wisdom. The Spirits of Wisdom are the Auphanim <note>original Hebrew: *Auphanim*, meaning "Wheels."</note>, a name which signifies the "Wheels," because everything in Heaven acts like immense wheels spangled with stars. Their empire is that of harmony. They correspond to the Reason.

<summary>This page introduces a Kabbalistic fragment by Eliphaz Levi that explains the spiritual hierarchy of the universe, linking the Sephiroth and angelic orders to divine numbers and their demonic counterparts.</summary>
<keywords>Eliphaz Levi, Yetzirah, Sephiroth, Kether, Chaioth Ha-Qadesh, Chokmah, Auphanim, Kabbalah, angelic hierarchy, Thamiel</keywords>

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of the Sephiroth—the ten divine emanations of the Kabbalah—and their corresponding angelic and demonic orders. It describes the third through the eighth Sephiroth, listing their virtues, their psychological correspondences, and the "Shells" (evil spirits) that act as their adversaries.</meta>

<page-num>125</page-num>
<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

They <note>These are the adversaries of the second Sephira, *Chokmah* (Wisdom), which was discussed on the preceding page.</note> have for adversaries the *Chaigidel*, or the "Shells" <note>original: "Shells." In Kabbalah, the *Qlippoth* or "Shells" represent unbalanced or evil forces that act as the shadow of the divine emanations.</note> which attach themselves to material and lying appearances. Their chief—or rather their guide, for evil spirits obey no one—is *Beelzebub*, whose name signifies "the God of Flies," because flies haunt putrefying corpses.

The third number is three. The third Sephira is *Binah*, or Understanding.

The spirits of *Binah* are the *Aralim*, or the "Strong Ones." Their realm is the creation of ideas; they correspond to the activity and energy of thought.

They have for adversaries the *Satariel*, or the "Concealers," who are the demons of absurdity, intellectual laziness, and mystery. The chief of the *Satariel* is *Lucifuge* <note>Meaning "one who flees the light."</note>, who is falsely and ironically called *Lucifer* <note>Meaning "Light-bringer." This is an example of calling something by its opposite to avoid its influence.</note> (just as the *Eumenides* <note>original Greek: *Eumenides*, meaning "the Gracious Ones."</note>—who are actually the Furies—are called "the Gracious Ones" in Greek).

The fourth number is four. The fourth Sephira is *Gedulah* or *Chesed*, meaning Magnificence or Mercy.

The spirits of *Gedulah* are the *Chaschmalim*, or the "Luminous Ones." Their realm is that of kindness; they correspond to the imagination.

They have for adversaries the *Gamchicoth*, or the "Disturbers of Souls." The chief or guide of these demons is *Ashtaroth* or *Astarte*—the "impure Venus" of the Syrians—whom they represent with the head of a donkey or a bull and the breasts of a woman.

The fifth number is five. The fifth Sephira is *Geburah*, or Justice.

The spirits of *Geburah* are the *Seraphim*, or the spirits burning with zeal. Their realm is the punishment of crimes. They correspond to the faculty of comparing and of choosing.

They have for adversaries the *Golab*, or "Incendiaries," spirits of wrath and rebellion, whose chief is *Asmodeus*, whom they also call *Samael* the Black.

The sixth number is six. The sixth Sephira is *Tiphereth*, or Supreme Beauty.

The spirits of *Tiphereth* are the *Malachim*, or the "Kings." Their realm is that of universal harmony. They correspond to judgment.

They have for adversaries the *Tagaririm*, or "Disputers," whose chief is *Belphegor*.

The seventh number is seven. The seventh Sephira is *Netzach*, or Victory.

The spirits of *Netzach* are the *Elohim*, or the "Gods"—that is to say, the representatives of God. Their realm is that of progress and life; they correspond to the *sensorium* <note>The sensory system or the faculty of sensation.</note> or to sensitivity.

They have for adversaries the *Harab-Serapel*, or the "Ravens of Death," whose chief is *Baal*.

The eighth number is eight. The eighth Sephira is *Hod*, or Eternal Order.

The spirits of *Hod* are the *Beni-Elohim*, or "Sons of the Gods." Their realm is that of order; they correspond to the inner sense.

<summary>This page details Sephiroth three through eight, listing the angelic choir, the psychological faculty, and the demonic "Shell" associated with each. It illustrates the Kabbalistic concept of a balanced universe where every divine force has a corresponding dark adversary.</summary>
<keywords>Kabbalah, Sephiroth, angelic hierarchy, demonic hierarchy, Binah, Chesed, Geburah, Tiphereth, Netzach, Hod, Eliphaz Levi, Beelzebub, Asmodeus</keywords>

<page-num>126</page-num>
<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

They <note>Referring to the spirits of the eighth Sephira, *Hod* (Splendor), discussed on the previous page.</note> have for adversaries the *Samael*, or the deceivers <note>original: "jugglers." In this context, it refers to those who use trickery, false magic, or intellectual illusions.</note>, whose chief is *Adramelech*.

The ninth number is nine. The ninth Sephira is *Yesod* <note>original Hebrew: *Yesod*, meaning "Foundation."</note>, or the fundamental principle.

The spirits of *Yesod* are the Cherubim or Angels—those powers which fertilize the earth, and which are represented in Hebrew symbolism in the form of bulls. Their empire is that of fertility. They correspond to true ideas.

They have for adversaries the *Gamaliel* <note>original Hebrew: *Gamaliel*, meaning "Reward of God," used here to represent those who seek base rewards.</note>, or the obscene ones, whose Queen is *Lilith*, the demon of debauchery.

The tenth number is ten. The tenth Sephira is *Malkuth* <note>original Hebrew: *Malkuth*, meaning "Kingdom."</note>, or the kingdom of forms.

The spirits of *Malkuth* are the *Ischim* <note>original Hebrew: *Ischim*, meaning "Men" or "Souls of Fire."</note>, or the "virile ones"; they are the souls of the Saints whose chief is Moses. (Let us not forget that it is Solomon who speaks here.—Eliphaz Lévi <note>Eliphaz Lévi was a famous 19th-century French occultist who edited and commented on these traditional magical texts.</note>.)

They have for adversaries the wicked ones who obey *Nahema* <note>A demonic figure in Jewish mysticism, often associated with unholy beauty and temptation.</note>, the demon of impurity.

The wicked are symbolized by the five accursed nations whom Joshua was commanded to destroy.

Joshua (or *Jehoshua*, meaning "The Savior") is a symbol of the Messiah.

His name <note>The Hebrew name *Yehoshua* (Jesus).</note> is composed of the letters of the Divine Four-letter Name <note>original: "Tetragram." The sacred name Y-H-V-H.</note> changed into the Five-letter Name <note>original: "Pentagram." Referring to the "Pentagrammaton," Y-H-Sh-V-H, where the letter *Shin* is added to the center of the name of God to create the name of the Savior.</note> by adding the letter *Shin* (*see Figure 94*).

Each letter of this five-letter name represents a power of good attacked by the five accursed nations.

For the real history of the people of God is the symbolic legend of all humanity.

The five accursed nations are:

1.  The *Amalekites*, or the Aggressors;
2.  The *Geburim*, or the Violent Ones;
3.  The *Raphaim*, or the Cowards;
4.  The *Nephilim*, or the Voluptuous Ones;
5.  The *Anakim*, or the Anarchists.

The Anarchists are defeated by the letter *Yod*, which is the Scepter of the Father.

The Violent are defeated by the letter *He*, which is the Gentleness of the Mother.

The Cowards are defeated by the letter *Vav* <note>original: "Vau."</note>, which is the Sword of Michael and the process of creation through hard work and pain.

The Voluptuous are defeated by the second *He*, which represents the painful labor of the Mother in giving birth.

Lastly, the Aggressors are defeated by the letter *Shin* <note>original: "Schin."</note>, which is the Fire of the Lord and the balancing law of Justice.

<summary>This page concludes the description of the ten Sephiroth by detailing Yesod and Malkuth, their angelic orders, and their demonic counterparts. It provides an allegorical interpretation of the Hebrew name for the Messiah as a five-letter name that overcomes five specific types of moral and social evil.</summary>
<keywords>Yesod, Malkuth, Cherubim, Lilith, Nahema, Joshua, Messiah, Tetragrammaton, Pentagrammaton, Shin, Amalekites, Kabbalah, Eliphaz Lévi</keywords>

--- Page 153 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discussion of the Sephiroth and their adversaries by defining the nature of "perverse spirits" and false gods as psychological or philosophical states. It transitions into a fatherly instruction from Solomon to his son, Rehoboam, regarding the true nature of wisdom and the mastery of fear.</meta>

<page-num>127</page-num>

# THE KEY OF SOLOMON

The princes of the perverse spirits are the false gods whom people worship.

Hell, therefore, has no government other than that inevitable law which punishes wickedness and corrects error; for false gods exist only in the mistaken opinions of their worshippers.

Baal, Belphegor, Moloch, and Adramelech were once the idols of the Syrians—soulless idols that are now destroyed, and of whom only the names remain.

The true God has conquered all demons, just as Truth triumphs over Error. This change occurs within the opinions of humanity, and the wars of the Archangel Michael against Satan are symbols of the movement and progress of spirits.

The Devil is always a god of refusal <note>In this philosophical context, the "Devil" represents the spirit of negation, denial, or the refusal to acknowledge truth.</note>.

Accepted forms of idolatry are considered religions in their own time.

Outdated forms of idolatry are seen as superstitions and sacrileges.

The collection of phantoms currently in fashion serves as the "heaven" of the ignorant.

The dumping ground for phantoms that even Folly no longer desires is "hell."

But all of this exists only in the imagination of the common people <note>original: "the vulgar"—meaning the uneducated masses or those lacking spiritual insight.</note>.

For the wise, heaven is supreme reason, and hell is folly.

However, it must be understood that we use the word "heaven" here in the mystical sense, contrasting it with the word "hell."

To summon phantoms, one only needs to become intoxicated or to drive oneself mad; for phantoms are always the companions of drunkenness and dizziness.

The "phosphorus" <note>original: "phosphorus"—here used metaphorically to mean the light or animating energy of the mind.</note> of the imagination, when left to the whims of over-excited and diseased nerves, fills itself with monsters and absurd visions.

One can also achieve hallucinations by blurring the lines between waking and sleeping through the gradual use of narcotics; however, such actions are crimes against nature.

Wisdom drives away phantoms and allows us to communicate with higher spirits by contemplating the laws of nature and studying sacred numbers.

->(Here King Solomon addresses his son, Rehoboam <note>original: "Roboam."</note>):<-

"My son Rehoboam, remember that the fear of the Lord <note>original Hebrew: *Adonai*.</note> is only the beginning of wisdom.

Keep and protect those who lack understanding in this fear of the Lord, for it will secure and preserve my crown for you.

But learn to triumph over fear yourself through wisdom, and the spirits will descend from heaven to serve you.

I, Solomon, your father, King of Israel and of Palmyra, have sought and obtained as my portion the holy *Chokmah* <note>original Hebrew: *Chokmah*, meaning "Wisdom"—specifically the second Sephira on the Tree of Life.</note>, which is the wisdom of the Lord."

<summary>This page defines hell and false gods as products of human error and imagination, arguing that true wisdom consists of transcending fear and hallucinations. It concludes with Solomon advising his son to use the "fear of God" to rule the ignorant while personally mastering that fear through divine wisdom.</summary>

<keywords>Solomon, Rehoboam, Adonai, Chokmah, Baal, Belphegor, Moloch, Adramelech, Michael, Satan, Phantoms, Idolatry, Wisdom, Hell, Heaven</keywords>

--- Page 154 ---
<meta>This page concludes the mystical instructions attributed to King Solomon, transitioning from philosophical definitions of heaven and hell to the practical application of divine names and sacred geometry in the creation of talismans.</meta>

<page-num>128</page-num>

<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

And I have become King of the Spirits of both Heaven and Earth, Master of those who dwell in the air and of the living souls of the sea, because I possessed the Key to the Hidden Gates of Light.

I have accomplished great things through the power of the *Shem HaMephorash* <note>original: "Schema Hamphorasch"; the "Explicit Name" of God, which in Kabbalistic tradition consists of 72 names derived from the Book of Exodus.</note> and through the Thirty-two Paths of *Yetzirah* <note>original: "Yetzirah"; meaning "Formation," referring to one of the four worlds in Kabbalah and a foundational text of Jewish mysticism, the Sefer Yetzirah.</note>.

Number, weight, and measure determine the form of all things; the substance is one, and God creates it eternally.

Happy is the one who understands the Letters and the Numbers.

The Letters come from the Numbers, the Numbers from Ideas, the Ideas from Forces, and the Forces from the *Elohim* <note>original: "Elohim"; a Hebrew name for God, often interpreted in this context as the various divine powers or creative attributes of the universe.</note>. The synthesis <note>the unified whole</note> of the *Elohim* is the *Schema* <note>the divine Name or Plan.</note>.

The *Schema* is one, its columns are two, its power is three, and its form is four; its reflection results in eight, which, when multiplied by three, gives you the twenty-four Thrones of Wisdom.

Upon each Throne rests a Crown with three rays. Each ray bears a Name, and each Name is an absolute idea. There are seventy-two Names upon the twenty-four Crowns of the *Schema*.

You shall write these Names upon thirty-six talismans—two upon each talisman, one on each side.

You shall divide these talismans into four series of nine each, according to the number of letters in the *Schema* <note>Referring to the four letters of the Tetragrammaton, the holy name of God (YHVH).</note>.

Upon the first series, you shall engrave the letter *Yod*, symbolized by the flowering rod of Aaron.

Upon the second, the letter *He*, symbolized by the cup of Joseph.

Upon the third, the letter *Vav*, symbolized by the sword of my father, David.

And upon the fourth, the final *He*, symbolized by the gold shekel <note>a unit of currency and weight in ancient Israel.</note>.

These thirty-six talismans will form a book containing all the secrets of Nature. By combining them in different ways, you shall make the spirits <note>original: "Genii"; guardian spirits or supernatural beings.</note> and angels speak.

---

->HERE ENDS THE FRAGMENT OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON.<-

<summary>This page concludes a fragment of the Key of Solomon, outlining a Kabbalistic system where numbers, letters, and divine names are used to create thirty-six magical talismans. It explains how these tools allow the practitioner to master the elements and communicate with celestial beings.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the visual mystical alphabets to a spoken ritual practice. It presents a specific invocation meant to align the practitioner with the various spheres and angelic orders of the Kabbalistic Tree of Life, as organized by the 19th-century occultist Eliphaz Lévi.</meta>

<header>THE KEY OF SOLOMON.</header>

# THE KABBALISTIC INVOCATION OF SOLOMON.

->*Given by Eliphaz Lévi in "Ritual of High Magic," <note>original: "Rituel de la Haute Magie"</note> Chapter 13.*<-

---

POWERS of the Kingdom <term>Malkuth</term>, be beneath my left foot and within my right hand.

Glory <term>Hod</term> and Eternity <term>Netzach</term> touch my shoulders and guide me in the Paths of Victory.

Mercy <term>Chesed</term> and Justice <term>Gevurah</term>, be the equilibrium and splendor of my life.
Understanding <term>Binah</term> and Wisdom <term>Chokmah</term>, give the Crown <term>Kether</term> to me.
Spirits of the Kingdom <term>Malkuth</term>, conduct me between the two columns <note>a reference to the pillars Jachin and Boaz in Solomon's Temple</note> upon which the entire structure of the Temple is supported.
Angels of Victory <term>Netzach</term> and of Glory <term>Hod</term>, strengthen me upon the Cubical Stone of the Foundation <term>Yesod</term>.

O GEDULAHEL <note>an angelic form of Gedulah, another name for Mercy/Chesed</note>! O GEBURAHEL <note>an angelic form of Gevurah/Justice</note>! O TIPHERETH <note>the sphere of Beauty and the Heart</note>!
BINAHEL <note>an angelic form of Binah/Understanding</note>, be my Love!
RUACH CHOKMAHEL <note>Spirit of Wisdom</note>, be my Light!
Be that which You are, and that which You will to be, O KETHERIEL <note>an angelic form of Kether/the Crown</note>!

Souls of Fire <term>Ishim</term>, assist me in the Name of the Almighty <term>Shaddai</term>.
Cherubim, be my strength in the Name of the Lord <term>Adonai</term>.
Sons of the Gods <term>Beni Elohim</term>, be my brothers in the Name of the Son, and by the virtues of the Hosts <term>Tzabaoth</term>.
Gods <term>Elohim</term>, fight for me in the Name of the Four-Lettered Name <term>Tetragrammaton</term>.
Angels <term>Malachim</term>, protect me in the Name of YOD HE VAU HE <note>the phonetic spelling of the Tetragrammaton, YHWH</note>.
Seraphim <note>the "burning ones"</note>, purify my love in the Name of God <term>Eloah</term>.
Brilliant Ones <term>Chaschmalim</term>, enlighten me with the splendors of God <term>Elohi</term> and of the Divine Presence <term>Shekhinah</term>.
Thrones <term>Aralim</term>, act; Wheels <term>Auphanim</term>, revolve and shine.
Holy Living Creatures <term>Chaioth Ha-Qadosch</term>, cry aloud, speak, roar, and groan; Holy, Holy, Holy <note>original: "Qadosch"</note>, Almighty, Lord, YOD CHAVAH, I AM THAT I AM <note>original: "Eheieh Asher Eheieh"</note>!

Praise Yah <note>original: "Halelu-Yah"</note>! Praise Yah! Praise Yah. Amen.

<summary>This page contains a ritual prayer known as the Kabbalistic Invocation of Solomon, which petitions the various divine names and angelic hierarchies associated with the Sephiroth (the spheres of the Tree of Life). It is presented as a method for the practitioner to achieve spiritual balance and divine protection.</summary>

<meta>This page contains a modernization of a manuscript treatise based on the biblical Song of Solomon, specifically chapters 5 and 6. The text uses the poetic imagery of the King James Version style to describe the physical and spiritual beauty of the "Beloved" and the "Bride."</meta>

# THE ESSENTIAL DESCRIPTION OF SOLOMON

->From a Manuscript Treatise on the Song of Songs, Chapter 8<-

What is your beloved, O you fairest among women? My beloved is radiant and ruddy <note>original: ruddy; meaning having a healthy, reddish complexion</note>, the most outstanding among ten thousand. His head is like the finest gold; his hair is wavy <note>original: bushy</note> and as black as a raven. His eyes are like the eyes of doves by the rivers of water, washed with milk and perfectly set. His cheeks are like a bed of spices, like sweet flowers; his lips are like lilies, dripping with liquid myrrh. His hands are like gold rings set with beryl; his body <note>original: belly; used here to refer to the torso or midsection</note> is like polished ivory overlaid with sapphires. His legs are like pillars of marble set upon bases of fine gold; his appearance is like Lebanon, as majestic as the cedars. His mouth is most sweet; yes, he is altogether lovely. This is my beloved, and this is my friend, O daughters of Jerusalem.

What makes your beloved better than any other beloved, O you fairest among women? What makes your beloved better than another, that you charge us so strictly? I charge you, O daughters of Jerusalem, if you find my beloved, tell him that I am lovesick <note>original: sick of love; meaning faint or overwhelmed by longing</note>.

Where has your beloved gone, O you fairest among women? Where has your beloved turned away, so that we may seek him with you? My beloved has gone down into his garden, to the beds of spices, to graze in the gardens and to gather lilies. I am my beloved’s, and my beloved is mine; he grazes among the lilies.

You are beautiful, O my love, as Tirzah <note>Tirzah was a beautiful city and an early capital of the Northern Kingdom of Israel</note>, as lovely as Jerusalem, as awe-inspiring <note>original: terrible; in the sense of inspiring great awe or terror</note> as an army with banners. Turn your eyes away from me, for they have overwhelmed me. Your hair is like a flock of goats appearing from Gilead. Your teeth are like a flock of sheep coming up from the washing, where every one has twins, and not one among them is childless. Like a slice of a pomegranate are your temples behind your veil <note>original: locks</note>. There are sixty queens, and eighty concubines, and young women <note>original: virgins</note> without number. My dove, my perfect one, is unique; she is the only daughter of her mother, the favorite of the one who gave her birth. The daughters saw her and called her blessed; yes, the queens and the concubines also praised her.

Who is she who looks out like the morning, as fair as the moon, as bright as the sun, and as awe-inspiring as an army with banners? I went down into the nut orchard to see the fruits of the valley, and to see whether the vine flourished and the pomegranates were in bloom. Before I realized it, my soul made me like the chariots of my noble people <note>original: Amminadib; a Hebrew phrase often interpreted as "my noble people" or a specific person's name</note>. Return, return, O Shulammite; return, return, that we may look upon you. What will you see in the Shulammite? Something like the dance of two armies.

<summary>This page provides a poetic modernization of the Song of Solomon, featuring the famous "blazon" or physical descriptions of the beloved and the bride. It emphasizes the mutual devotion and overwhelming beauty of the two figures using lush, topographical metaphors.</summary>
<keywords>Song of Solomon, Shulammite, biblical poetry, Solomon, allegory, Tirzah, Jerusalem, Amminadib, beauty</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the poetic description of the biblical figure of Solomon, drawing from the Song of Songs (Canticles) to depict the "Beloved" as an ideal of physical and spiritual beauty. In the context of the "Key of Solomon," this biblical imagery is often used to establish the divine authority and character of the legendary King Solomon before transitioning into ritual or magical instructions.</meta>

# <insert>The Greater Key Of Solomon</insert>

<vocab>The Greater Key of Solomon, The Key of Solomon (Clavicula Salomonis), Solomon</vocab>

# THE ESSENTIAL DESCRIPTION OF SOLOMON

->From a Manuscript Treatise on the Song of Songs, Chapter 8<-

What makes your beloved better than any other beloved, O you fairest among women? My beloved is radiant and ruddy <note>original: ruddy; meaning having a healthy, reddish complexion</note>, the most outstanding among ten thousand. His head is like the finest gold; his hair is wavy <note>original: bushy; describing thick, curled hair</note> and as black as a raven. His eyes are like the eyes of doves by the rivers of water, washed with milk and perfectly set. His cheeks are like a bed of spices, like sweet flowers; his lips are like lilies, dripping with liquid myrrh. His hands are like gold rings set with beryl; his body <note>original: belly; in Early Modern English, this referred to the entire torso or midsection</note> is like polished ivory overlaid with sapphires. His legs are like pillars of marble set upon bases of fine gold; his appearance is like Lebanon, as majestic as the cedars. His mouth is most sweet; yes, he is altogether lovely. This is my beloved, and this is my friend, O daughters of Jerusalem.

What makes your beloved better than any other beloved, O you fairest among women? What makes your beloved better than another, that you charge us so strictly? I charge you, O daughters of Jerusalem, if you find my beloved, tell him that I am lovesick <note>original: sick of love; meaning faint or overwhelmed by longing and desire</note>.

Where has your beloved gone, O you fairest among women? Where has your beloved turned away, so that we may seek him with you? My beloved has gone down into his garden, to the beds of spices, to graze <note>original: feed; in the sense of a shepherd tending a flock or someone enjoying the fruits of a garden</note> in the gardens and to gather lilies. I am my beloved’s, and my beloved is mine; he grazes among the lilies.

You are beautiful, O my love...

<summary>This page presents a poetic description of Solomon using imagery from the biblical Song of Solomon, establishing him as a figure of divine beauty and perfection. It frames the King in a mystical light, likely as a preamble to the magical knowledge attributed to him in the manuscript.</summary>
